## D21-76 · A re-entered walk replays its journaled control decisions and resumes where it stopped; only new work re-executes

**Taken 2026-09-27 by the operator, in the session executing `s8d`, on a brief with four options and a recommendation
(option (a)).**

**Why.** `s8d` refuting round 7 measured a conflict between two parts of the stage's own specification. §8d.2 (and
ADR-0174 decision 7) re-walk from the top on re-entry: committed agent steps are served, but `check` steps and
idempotent `tool` steps re-execute and later refs bind the re-executed values. Schema §3 and §8d.3's escalate re-entry
say an answered escalation *"resumes **into the same step**"*. In `tdd-red-green-refactor` (both lanes) `green` changes
the tree before `green-gate` (`tdd.green-exhausted`) opens; on the answer's re-walk `red-verify` re-runs over the
partly fixed tests, `red-check` (`report.failed > 0 and report.passed == 0`) no longer holds, and the walk loops back
into `red`. A probe of the same mechanism measured two extra dispatches and a failed activation. Both refuters graded
it a specification conflict for the operator, not a build defect.

**Ruled — option (a).** The walk **journals its control decisions** — each `check` verdict and each loop-back or
escalation decision — under the commit key of the frame that took it, and a re-entered walk **replays** them: up to
the last journaled decision it neither re-executes the deciding `check` nor re-decides a loop; from there, only work
the prior walk did not reach executes fresh (a crash re-entry therefore still re-verifies whatever it had not yet
decided). An answered escalate gate thus resumes into its escalating step. Carried by `s8d`, amending ADR-0174
decision 7 and ADR-0175 (both `proposed`) by dated text; red-first tests include a re-entry past an answered gate with
a changed `test` answer (no committed-frame step re-dispatched; the walk resumes after the gate) and a tdd-shaped
escalate re-entry that dispatches `green`, not `red`.

**Declined.** (b) committing idempotent tool outputs a later check reads (a crash re-entry would stop re-verifying the
tree); (c) owing it to `s8e`; (d) accepting the re-walk as designed.

---

## D21-77 · A journaled refusal is not replayed; decision rows do not draw on the retry cap; one focused round past s8d's cap

**Taken 2026-09-27 by the operator, in the session executing `s8d`, on refuting round 8's findings (the cap).**

**Why.** Round 8 found, in D21-76's build: (i) a journaled `false` whose route is a refusal (`"fail"`, no `on_false`, or
an exhausted `{to, max}` loop-back) is replayed on every later run, so a refused activation cannot complete after the
tree is fixed until its activity version changes (the records said *"a replayed false re-takes the same route"*; both
refuters graded it a residual, not a spec violation); (ii) decision rows take activation attempt numbers, which
`retry::decide` compares against the pack's AC-029 `attempts_cap`, so journaled decisions consume a later agent step's
retries (confirmed major); (iii) a gate `when` that decides `false` is journaled by no test (confirmed major).

**Ruled.**
1. **Replay only decisions whose route continues the walk** — a `true`, a loop-back, a `repeat` pass, an escalation.
   A `false` routed to a refusal is **not replayed**: the next run re-verifies it (the deciding `check` re-executes over
   re-executed tool output). ADR-0174 decision 7 and ADR-0175 (both `proposed`) are amended by dated text; a test pins a
   refused-then-fixed re-run completing.
2. **(ii) and (iii) are fixed** red-first with mutants: decision rows draw no attempt from AC-029's cap (the retry cap
   counts dispatch attempts only), and a gate `when` deciding `false` is journaled and replayed under a killing test.
3. **One focused refuting round (9) past the cap of 8**, over this fix's diff only, before the formal Checkpoint 8d.

---

## D21-78 · `s8d` closes: agent steps commit at step grain, the reference resolver is complete, `on_false` iterates, the kernel writes the error row, and selection chooses by rank

**Taken 2026-09-27 by the session executing `s8d`, at the stage's close.** It records and cites. It re-takes no ruling
and writes no go-ahead line. Records R1–R8 flip under D21-64 ruling 10, each by its own dated ledger line (D21-79 …
D21-86), not by this entry. `s8d` names no D-entry section of its own (the word appears nowhere in its file), so this
entry takes D21-68's form, and what it records is what `s8d` §8d.1, §8d.3a, §8d.9, §8d.11 and §8d.13 record. Every
figure below is quoted from the stage file's recorded readings, the tree or the committed records; none is new here.

Records: R1 = ADR-0174, R2 = ADR-0173, R3 = ADR-0175, R4 = ADR-0176, R5 = ADR-0177, R6 = ADR-0178, R7 = ADR-0172,
R8 = ADR-0179

**Recorded — the eight records, and how they flip.** Each id was recorded at write time by a dated line in §8d.1
(D21-47); each record is `status: proposed` at `b183eb9`, in ADR-0164's form, and `check-adr-options.py` scans 16
records from 0164 and flags 0. The README's next free number reads **`0180`**.
- **R7 = ADR-0172** (D21-67 ruling 2, an entry prerequisite): *a comparison against the literal `null` is a definite
  presence test*. Supersedes in part ADR-0103 decision 4 and ADR-0121 decision 3's first bullet, for `==`/`!=`
  against the literal only (`in` stands).
- **R2 = ADR-0173**: *the complete reference resolver*, one mechanism, `runtime::reference`. Discharges ADR-0136
  clause 4 and ADR-0142 clause 4; supersedes in part ADR-0042's Decision and first forbids bullet (D21-48 X8) and
  ADR-0148 decisions 1, 3 and 4. Amended before acceptance by D21-74 ruling 2 (decision 9a).
- **R1 = ADR-0174**: *step-grain commits*. Supersedes in part ADR-0143 (decision 2 narrowed, decision 4 in full),
  ADR-0150 (decision 1 in full; decision 2 and forbids bullets 1 and 4 in part), ADR-0138's grain sentence, ADR-0140
  (for per-step specs, decision 5 and the third forbids bullet; for agent steps, decision 1's withdrawal, decision 4
  and the second forbids bullet; R1FIX-8d and R2FIX-8d), ADR-0171's sixth forbids bullet's first half and ADR-0099
  decisions 1 and 2 as to *per-activity*, both for per-step specs. Discharges ADR-0143 clause 5 and ADR-0150 clause 4.
  Amended before acceptance by D21-76 and D21-77 (decision 7).
- **R3 = ADR-0175**: *`on_false` as iteration*. Executes ADR-0031, ADR-0044, ADR-0103 clause 6 and ADR-0144; names
  ADR-0046's `deviate` route OWED (D21-48 X7's narrowing of D21-42 4d); supersedes nothing. Decision 7's charge per
  frame was settled by accepted ADR-0122 (`290f0dd`); decision 6 was amended by D21-76 and D21-77.
- **R4 = ADR-0176**: *the kernel writes the error row, and the breaker refuses from it*. `at` and `family` are members
  added to ADR-0095 decision 2's arm, no `EventKind` is minted; supersedes nothing.
- **R5 = ADR-0177**: *two review verb rows and the question registry*. `review_clone` and `review_confirm` join
  ADR-0170 decision 1's closed route set by record; supersedes nothing.
- **R6 = ADR-0178**: *the `change` deriver in `build_item`, and `change` at triage* (D21-43 N3); a dated AC-079
  reading note; supersedes nothing.
- **R8 = ADR-0179** (D21-73 option (C)): *selection chooses by rank, builds one node per activity and binds `rigor`,
  and a `toolchain` row's undeclared inputs are supplied*. Supersedes in part ADR-0170 decision 9, at *"(`s8g` writes
  this repository's set)"* only. Amended before acceptance by D21-74 ruling 1 (decisions 2a, 2b).
- **They flip at this close under D21-64 ruling 10:** *"Standing authority for the rest of this session (`s8d`
  onward): each stage's own records flip at that stage's close under the same two conditions, each with its own dated
  ledger line citing this entry."* The conditions are ruling 9's, read for this stage: the formal Checkpoint 8d passes
  whole, and a refuting round finds no blocker or major. Refuting round 9 was dry (below); the formal reading is the
  last paragraph of this entry. One dated line per record follows the ruling's letter.

**Recorded — the provisional and in-session rulings this stage took.** D21-72 (option (B): the test `FakeHarness`
appends ADR-0126's `dispatch_completed` on an `Err` or cancelled dispatch only); D21-73 (option (C): `s8d` fixes
selection steps 2, 4 and 5 under R8 and supplies the `sca_scan`/`package` row inputs; its item 4 is owed to
`s8g`/`s8h`, and `bisect`'s registry row to the stage that owns its verb); D21-74 (no step-5 self-edge; `"affected"`
inside `Changeset`'s producer reads the run branch's diff); D21-75 (Checkpoint 8d (6a)'s floors set to the measured
fix 10 and story 8); D21-76 (a re-entered walk replays its journaled control decisions and resumes where it
stopped); D21-77 (a refusal is not replayed; decision rows draw nothing from AC-029's retry cap; one focused round 9
past the cap of 8). All six were taken by the operator in the session, or written as one of them directs (D21-75).

**Recorded — what was built** (`d647e88` P1–P8, `bfe0324`, `8107640`, and the refuting-round fixes `429177d` …
`b183eb9`).
- **Step-grain commits (R1).** Each agent step dispatches under its own per-step spec on `RecordedSpec`, carrying its
  own `timeout_secs` bounded by the activity budget, and commits as one `activity_committed` row carrying `step_id`
  and `iteration_path` under the separate commit key `(item, activity@version, step_id, iteration path)`;
  `IdempotencyKey` keeps three parts (N15). An activation completes on its `emit` row; re-entry serves every
  committed step activation and resumes at the first uncommitted one, across a real process death
  (`cross_process::kill_and_resume_n_step`). A `side_effecting` column on every registry row makes such a tool step
  commit and be served.
- **The complete resolver (R2).** Every ADR-0040 source form resolves or refuses by name; feedback refs bind the
  previous frame, `??` coalesces, `[*]` concatenates in element order (X5), a `repeat.until` over a later step runs
  its body (X6); `kb:`, `checklist:` and feed keys resolve through `s8c`'s tables and an undeclared key refuses even
  with `?`; every resolved `artifact:` input makes one call to a non-emitting read seam (`s8f` owns `artifact.read`);
  `check`, `emit` and test selection decide from resolved values; `selection_of` has one body, in `rk-kernel`. The
  forward-ref licence lint is widened (X8): 76 refs after the four unlicensed ones were removed under ADR-0072.
- **Iteration and `on_false` (R3).** `for_each`, `repeat` and `{ to, max }` loop-backs run under their own frames;
  exhaustion, `"fail"` or no `on_false` refuses by name (`StepFailedAfterCommit` after a commit), committed steps
  standing; each loop-back and `repeat` frame is charged per dispatch (m14, under ADR-0122).
- **The kernel-written error row and the breaker (R4).** One `step_failed` / `step_cancelled` / `step_refused` /
  `rate_limited` row per failed dispatch, written through `StepError::event()` whatever the harness; the executor
  folds the store's family-scoped rows with the pack's `K` and refuses an open family before the port (AC-033..035).
  Design §5.4's row and `runtime.rs`'s *"AC-033's breaker mark \| none exists"* row were corrected in the same change.
- **The review verb rows and the question registry (R5).** `shallow_clone` and `verdict_record` are rowed and routed,
  both `side_effecting`; `.redkiln/processes/questions.toml` holds the nine lane questions and N3's `facet.change`,
  loaded by one non-recursive loader for `run` and `answer`.
- **The `change` deriver (R6).** `R-change-extension-and-numstat-marker` runs at `build_item` step (7) over
  `HEAD..rk-run/<item>`; a record-supplied derived value is refused; a `null` `change` at the lane's first stage opens
  AC-079's `unknown_facet:change` gate, and the answer binds the facet.
- **Selection (R8).** Step 4 chooses by `(method_rank, activity_id)`, never the derived `Ord` (an equal lowest rank
  still `RankTie`); step 5 builds one node per activity and draws no self-edge (D21-74); step 2 binds `rigor` for
  `require` rows; `package_binaries` and `sca_scan`'s `audit_db`/`deny_db` are supplied from the run's row inputs.
- **D21-67's rulings (§8d.3a).** (a) R7 landed first, with its evaluator change in `compare`. (b) The 39
  severity-only rows are disposed of by class, with 39 version bumps and changelog rows under ADR-0072 (42 rows with
  X8's), and `checks=92 refused=0 indeterminate=0`. (c) AC-527's refusal 2 widens to *not `true`*, with two fixtures,
  mutant D67-5 killed, and a dated AC-527 amendment. (d) `rk-kernel`'s lib.rs sentence takes its dated correction.
- **Decision journaling (D21-76, D21-77).** A re-entered walk replays its journaled control decisions (a `check`'s
  verdict, a `repeat`'s `until`, a gate's `when`) from decision rows on `activity_committed`, which are not commits;
  only a decision whose route continues the walk is journaled, so a refused activation re-verifies on the next run
  and a fixed tree completes; decision rows draw nothing from AC-029's cap.
- **The walk table (§8d.9).** `LANE_WALK fix walked=10 refused=5` and `LANE_WALK story walked=8 refused=6`; no stage's
  selection refuses on either lane; every remaining refusal is owed to `s8f` or `s8g` by dated receiver note (D21-75).

## D21-87 · ADR-0180 is written `proposed` — the run-owned worktree lives at `.redkiln/worktrees/<item>` under the main worktree, is reused on re-entry and never force-removed, and is guarded

**Written 2026-09-27 in the session executing `s8d-ii`, at its build (package P1-RECORD), under the operator's AFK
rule.** A record entry, not a ruling and not the stage's close D-entry: it names the one record `s8d-ii`'s records
table requires, with the id it took at writing, so that Checkpoint 8d-ii locates it by `REC_WT` (D21-47: *"Records are
located by the id recorded at write time"*). It flips nothing and writes no go-ahead line.

**The record is ADR-0180**, `references/adr/0180-the-run-worktree-lives-in-dot-redkiln-is-reused-and-guarded.md`,
`status: proposed`, `date: 2026-09-27`, in ADR-0085's `### Option — <name>` form (`check-adr-options.py` scans 17
records from 0164 and flags 0). It executes D21-44, D21-45 W1, W2, W4 and W3's carry, D21-46's two corrections and R8,
D21-47 V1 and its clause-5 rule, and D21-48 X1 (*"**Refuse by name.** This answers D21-46's flip-line flag."*), X2 (the
post-checkout pointer scan) and X14 (the carry at creation). It takes no decision those entries did not take.

**Supersedes in part**, each quoted in the record: ADR-0137 decision 3's path literal,
*"`<common-dir>/redkiln/worktrees/<item>`"* (its rule, *"**`project_root` is the run-owned worktree**"*, stands);
ADR-0105 clause 6, *"A worktree is **re-created**, never reused, per run"*, and the *"a reused worktree"* member of its
forbids bullet, **for the run-owned worktree only** (`redkiln-migrate`'s worktree keeps clause 6, as AC-522 verifies);
ADR-0105 clause 5's closed list (*"Anything not listed is a successor."*) for the **writes** lock, unlock, the
`RK_BASE` marker, `reset --hard HEAD` and `clean -fd`, the reads superseding nothing; and ADR-0107 decision 2, *"a second
`init` on an initialised repository changes nothing and exits 0"*, for the first `init` after the record on a
repository initialised before it. **Cited and not superseded:** ADR-0018 (it names no worktree path) and ADR-0162
decision 10 (the pass *"removes its own reference worktree on every path, including every refusal path"*; its contrast
clause names `recreate_run_worktree`, which the record retires, and the record says so).

ADR-0137 *"are **not** written with the record. They move in the flip commit"*. `references/adr/README.md`'s supersession

(**`0181`**) and record census (180 over `0001`–`0180`) moved with the record, as did `runbook/00-preamble.md`'s.

round is dry, on its own dated ledger line; the flip commit writes the three back-links. Checkpoint 8d-ii (9) asserts
whichever back-link state the record's status requires, so it passes over either state and needs no D21-49 flip-stop
of its own. Until then the record binds nothing.

## D21-88 · `s8d-ii` findings 17 and 18: a run tree's untracked links are unlinked before its unforced removal, and a fresh tree whose carry or setup fails is rolled back

**Taken 2026-09-27 by the operator, in the session executing `s8d-ii`, on a brief with measured options and a
recommendation for each.**

**Why.** (17) Measured on git 2.55.0.windows.3: after `setup` ran `pnpm install` in a run tree, git's unforced
`git worktree remove` deregistered it but left 114 dangling junctions on disk; `rk_git::worktree::remove_landed`, which
unlinks every UNTRACKED symlink or junction inside a clean, landed, marked, unlocked tree without following it and
then runs the unforced removal, left nothing. (18) A fresh tree whose carry or `setup` failed stayed registered; the
next run re-entered it as `Creation::Reused`, and §8d-ii.4's *"A reused tree is not re-carried"* skipped both the
carry and `setup`, so the run would proceed over half its dependencies.

**Ruled.**
1. **(17) Keep the pre-unlink as built:** a link inside the tree is removed, never its target, then git's unforced
   removal runs, and any residue refuses as `WorktreeRemoveFailed`. It executes ADR-0180 decision 9 as written; the
   measurement is added to decision 9's evidence before the flip.
2. **(18) Roll back on failure:** when a FRESH tree's carry or `setup` fails, the tree is removed (unforced, through the
   same pre-unlink path) before the run refuses by name, as D21-48 X2's pointer scan already does, so the next run
   creates it anew and re-carries. A reused tree is still not re-carried. Built red-first in `s8d-ii`, recorded in
   ADR-0180 (`proposed`).

---

## D21-89 · A run tree re-created on an existing run branch is checked, and based, at what it actually checks out: the filter read, the pointer scan and the length rule read the branch tip, and `RK_BASE` records the merge-base

**Taken 2026-09-28 by the operator, in the session executing `s8d-ii`, on refuting round 1's findings (the base choice
was the operator's; the check-the-checked-out-commit fix follows from D21-48 X2 and V1's intent).**

**Why.** When the run branch exists and no tree is registered (routine after a D21-88 ruling-2 rollback), `create`
reads `fork` from `start`'s HEAD and runs the V1 filter read, the X2 pointer scan's path set and the length rule over
it, while the tree checks out the branch tip; a probe created a tree holding a pointer file with no refusal. `RK_BASE`
also recorded that HEAD as the fork, so a review reference tree would diff against the wrong base (§8d-ii.8 had named
the base choice for *"the reference-tree package"*).

**Ruled.** On the existing-branch path: (1) the filter read, the pointer scan's path set and the length rule read the
**commit being checked out** (the run branch's tip, read before the add); (2) `RK_BASE` records
**`merge-base(start HEAD, run-branch tip)`** — the point the branch forked. First creation is unchanged (fork = start's
HEAD). Built red-first in `s8d-ii`, stated in ADR-0180 (`proposed`).

---

## D21-91 · ADR-0180 flips to `accepted` — the run-owned worktree lives at `.redkiln/worktrees/<item>` under the main worktree, is reused on re-entry and never force-removed, and is guarded

**Confirmed 2026-09-28 by the operator, in the session executing `s8d-ii`, under D21-64 ruling 10.** ADR-0180
(`s8d-ii`'s record, D21-87) flips `proposed → accepted`, `date: 2026-09-27` unchanged. It was written `proposed` at
`s8d-ii`'s build and revised in place during that stage while it bound nothing (D21-88, D21-89 and refuting rounds 1
and 2). The ruling reads:
*"Standing authority for the rest of this session (`s8d` onward): each stage's own records flip at that stage's close
under the same two conditions, each with its own dated ledger line citing this entry. This lifts D21-49's flip-stops
for those records."* The two conditions are ruling 9's, *"if the formal Checkpoint 8c passes whole and a refuting
round finds no blocker or major"*, read for this stage's Checkpoint 8d-ii.

Both conditions hold. The formal Checkpoint 8d-ii passed whole at `497266b` (D21-90, its last paragraph: rc 0, 0
`FAIL`, `CP8D-II-END-OF-BLOCK reached`, the extracted block 536 lines; (4)'s whole suite 1034 passed / 0 failed / 10
ignored over 44 result lines). Refuting round 2 (`s8d-ii` §8d-ii.9, findings 28 to 35) was dry: no blocker, no major.

The text that flips is the one at `497266b`; the flip commit changes only its `status` line. No Decision clause and no
forbids bullet moves. The supersession in part of ADR-0137 decision 3's path literal,
*"`<common-dir>/redkiln/worktrees/<item>`"* (its rule, *"**`project_root` is the run-owned worktree**"*, stands), of
ADR-0105 clause 6's *"A worktree is **re-created**, never reused, per run"* and the *"a reused worktree"* member of
its forbids bullet, for the run-owned worktree only (`redkiln-migrate`'s worktree keeps clause 6 whole), of ADR-0105
clause 5's closed write list for the writes lock, unlock, the `RK_BASE` marker, `reset --hard` and `clean -fd` (the
reads supersede nothing), and of ADR-0107 decision 2 for the first `init` after this record on a repository
initialised before it, is now in force; all three stay `accepted`. **The flip commit writes the three `superseded_by`
back-links**, `0180` appended to ADR-0105's, ADR-0107's and ADR-0137's lists, as `s8d-ii`'s records table requires
(*"They move in the flip commit"*). ADR-0018 and ADR-0162 decision 10 are cited and not superseded. D21-88 rulings 1
and 2 and D21-89 are the rulings it carries. **Checkpoint 8d-ii is re-run after this flip commit**, and its (9) then
asserts all three back-links.

## D21-92 · A routing `null` is journaled when its route continues the walk; a `null` routed to a refusal is not

**Taken 2026-09-28 by the operator, in the session executing `s8e` (Weigh-In `wi-a0f617`, option B), on a card with
three grounded options and a recommendation.**

**Why.** `s8d` §8d.13 left one question *"owed to the operator"*: *"A `null` `until` or gate `when` is never
journaled. A re-entry therefore re-evaluates it even when it sits before a later journaled decision, and over
re-executed tool output it can route differently there."* ADR-0174 decision 7 (accepted, D21-81) names the residual.

**Ruled.** A `null` `until` or gate `when` is journaled on the same rule as a `true` or `false`: **when its route
continues the walk**. A `null` routed to a refusal leaves no row, so a refused-then-fixed re-run still completes
(D21-77). Built red-first with mutants in `s8e`, stated in a new record written `proposed` that supersedes ADR-0174
decision 7 in part (its null sentence only); the test `a_null_until_or_gate_when_is_not_journaled` is replaced by
tests for both arms, by dated edit to Checkpoint 8d's selection where it names it.

---

## D21-93 · An approved seal is scoped to the stage it was made in: `terminal()` reads `Sealed` only until the next stage transition

**Taken 2026-09-28 by the operator, in the session executing `s8e`, on `s8e` §8e.0 item 8's STOP (option 3 of three,
the recommendation).**

**Why.** Package S8E-P0 measured, in the test binary: `fsm::terminal()` checks `sealed` first and nothing clears it,
so `fold(review -> slice_sealed{approved} -> release -> gate_opened)` gives `awaiting_gate = Some(..)` but
`terminal = {"terminal":"sealed","verdict":"approved"}`. `replay::fsm_residue` feeds the run verb's `run.json`
`terminal_state`, folded over the whole prior journal, so every run after review would record `sealed`, one halted
at a release gate included, and AC-008's terminal-state half could not tell a kernel idle after the seal from a
working one. The shipped packs `bug-standard` and `story` both run release, operate and closeout after review.
§8e.0 item 8 makes this a STOP until the slice record says how the fold scopes a seal.

**Ruled.** The fold records the stage a seal was made in, and `terminal()` returns `Sealed` only until a later
`stage_transition`; after it, `terminal()` falls through to gate, dispatch or complete. The `sealed` member keeps the
verdict as history, so resume still reads all three axes (ADR-0109 decision 3). Stated in `s8e`'s slice record
(`proposed`), built red-first with a mutant (the scope dropped) in §8e.8's package; no committed golden log moves (none
contains `slice_sealed`, S8E-P0).

---

## D21-94 · An undeclared slice on a fix lane is refused at entry, before any dispatch; three other `s8e` record choices confirmed

**Taken 2026-09-28 by the operator, in the session executing `s8e`, reviewing records ADR-0181 … ADR-0186 (all
`proposed`) during the build, on four questions with grounded options and a recommendation each.**

**Ruled — one change.** ADR-0186 decision 2 sited the refusal of an absent, empty or otherwise undeclarable `slice` at
the seal's mint, so a fix lane with no slice walks to its review stage, dispatches its review, and only then refuses
(*"The cost of the mint siting, stated"*). **The refusal moves to the lane's entry, before any dispatch**, for a lane
that will reach a review stage (the fix lane always does): an undeclared slice there is a certain failure, and it must
not be paid for. The mint keeps its refusal as the backstop. Built red-first with a mutant (the entry check dropped) in
`s8e`; ADR-0186 (`proposed`) is amended by dated text, and its *"refuse an undeclared slice at entry"* option becomes
the one taken, stated as a divergence from ADR-0159 decision 2's mint siting for this key only.

**Confirmed — three record choices, as written:**
1. ADR-0182 decision 4: under `ByItem(x)` a row that carries no key is returned, as the **interim** (the inclusive
   reading), with exclusion plus a named `doctor` finding taken once every producer of a `Carried` kind keys its rows.
   This departs from `s8e` §8e.10's recommendation, for the reason the record gives.
2. ADR-0184 decisions 2 and 5: `expired` carries its action (`fail`, `escalate`, `auto-decide <verdict>`) by the same
   deleted-database test as `answered`'s option, and ADR-0109 decision 2's parenthetical is read as illustrative (not
   superseded).
3. ADR-0185 decision 3: with the database deleted, `answer` refuses fail-closed until the next `run` writes
   `worktree_assigned` again; ownership has one source.

## D21-95 · "Will reach a review stage" is read from declared `provides`, never from a stage name; the shipped catalog's missing `Findings` producer is owed to `s8g`

**Taken 2026-09-28 by the operator, in the session executing `s8e`, on package S8E-RULINGS's blocker (four grounded
options and a recommendation), and on the gap that blocker measured.**

**Why.** D21-94 refuses an undeclared slice at entry *"for a lane that will reach a review stage (the fix lane always
does)"*. Accepted ADR-0162 forbids *"matching `resolved.stage` against a string literal to find the review stage"*;
the review pass is selected by an activity's declared `provides`, and `review_pass` seals only when a committed
activation's schema is `Findings`. Measured by a `tomllib` census over `.redkiln/catalog/*.toml`: **0** shipped
activities declare `provides` first `Findings`; the shipped `code-review` provides `["ReviewVerdict"]`. So under
ADR-0162's rule no shipped lane reaches the seal, and `Slice-Verdict:` is minted over fixtures only.

**Ruled.**
1. **The entry check decides from declared `provides` (option A).** At entry it walks the stage order from the current
   stage through `Pack::successor`, skipping forbidden stages, and refuses by name, through the one validator
   `seal_act` also calls, when any stage reached has a catalog activity for that stage whose `provides` first is
   `Findings`. No stage-name literal; ADR-0162 stands whole. D21-94's parenthetical *"(the fix lane always does)"* is
   read as the operator's expectation, not as the test. The check is inert on the shipped packs until item 2 lands;
   the mint's refusal stays the backstop.
2. **The shipped catalog's missing `Findings` producer is owed to `s8g`** by a dated receiver note in `s8g`'s file: the
   shipped review activity and `review_pass`'s schema key must agree (either `code-review` provides `Findings`, or the
   pass keys on what the shipped review provides), decided by a record there, so a shipped lane reaches the seal.
   `s8e` closes on its fixtures and states the gap in ADR-0186.

---

## D21-96 · A tier-1 `answered` row stores the gate's bound tree, as its `Gate:` mark does; the answer's observed tree stays in the tier-2 void row

**Taken 2026-09-28 by the operator, in the session executing `s8e`, on package R1FIX-8e's blocker (defect (c)), four
grounded options and a recommendation (the writer side).**

**Why.** Walking `doctor::ac184_rebuild_differential_over_anchored_tier_one_rows`'s lane through a void fails the AC-184
differential: only-recorded `t1_gate_transition|63d28550…|answered|70566bf3…`, only-rebuilt `…|answered|e9ead599…`.
`rk-store`'s tier-one derivation takes the `answered` row's `tree_hash` from the `gate_answered` payload, which is the
answer's observed tree; ADR-0184 decision 1 fixes the `Gate:` value's `<tree_hash>` as *"the gate's bound tree (ADR-0105
clause 4), the same on every mark for the token"*, and the rebuild fills that. ADR-0181 decision 11 claims a rebuilt
row is *"the row the live act anchored to that commit"*; over a void it is not. For an applied answer the two trees are
equal, which is why nothing caught it.

**Ruled.** The live `answered` row of `t1_gate_transition` carries the gate's **bound** tree, taken from the gate record,
so the tier-1 row equals what its mark carries and a rebuild reproduces it byte for byte. The answer's observed tree is
not lost: it stays in the anchored tier-2 `gate_answer_void` row. ADR-0181 (`proposed`) is amended by dated text;
built red-first on the `ac184` lane extended through a void, with a mutant (the observed tree restored) killed.

## D21-97 · `s8e` closes: tier-1 writes are git-first through the kernel, the stage lives on the run ref, the lane crosses processes, and `Slice-Verdict:` is sealed on both review outcomes

**Taken 2026-09-28 by the session executing `s8e`, at the stage's close.** It records and cites. It re-takes no
ruling and writes no go-ahead line. The stage's seven records, ADR-0181 … ADR-0187, flip under D21-64 ruling 10, each
by its own dated ledger line (D21-98 … D21-104), not by this entry. `s8e` names no D-entry section of its own (its
attendant edits end *"a ledger entry"*), so this entry takes D21-90's form, and what it records is what `s8e`'s
records table, §8e.0, §8e.9, §8e.12, §8e.13, §8e.14 and §8e.15 record. Every figure below is quoted from the stage
file's recorded readings, the tree or the committed records; none is new here.

Records: the tier-1 writer = ADR-0181, the interim `item` key = ADR-0182, the stage on the run ref = ADR-0183, the
`Gate:` value = ADR-0184, the three tier-2 kinds = ADR-0185, the slice = ADR-0186, the routing `null` (D21-92) =
ADR-0187.

**Recorded — the seven records, and how they flip.** Each id was recorded at write time by a dated line in the
records table (D21-47: *"Records are located by the id recorded at write time"*); each record is `status: proposed`
at `492e516`, in ADR-0085's `### Option — <name>` form, and `check-adr-options.py` scans 24 records from 0164 and
flags 0. The README's next free number reads **`0188`**.
- **ADR-0181**: *the tier-1 writer is one method on `EventStore`, `append_tier1(event, &TierOneAnchor)`, and it is
  written git-first by the kernel*. **Supersedes in part** ADR-0158 decision 3 and its bullet *"Claiming AC-160,
  AC-161, AC-162 or AC-163 is met, on any axis"*, and ADR-0155 decision 5(b) and its bullet *"Stating or implying
  that AC-162 is satisfied for stage changes 2..N of a lane"*, *"only for the criteria `s8e` measures (§8e.14), which
  are all four … and only once they are measured"*. Cites ADR-0105 clause 5 and ADR-0158's `rk-cli` bullet without
  superseding them (D21-48 X10). Amended before acceptance by D21-96 (decision 3's dated bullet) and by S8E-P7's
  dated notes on decision 11.
- **ADR-0182**: *the interim `item` key rides beside the event, outside `payload` and outside `canonical_events`, and
  `query` honours `ByItem`*. Supersedes nothing: ADR-0131's first forbids bullet is honoured, because R3 overtakes
  N5's supersession (§8e.13 finding 12). Its decision 4 (the inclusive `ByItem` interim) is D21-94's confirmation 1.
- **ADR-0183**: *the stage lives on the run ref*. **Supersedes in part** ADR-0105 clause 3's `Story:` value
  delegation; ADR-0155 decisions 1, 2 and 5(a) and two of its forbids bullets; ADR-0157 decisions 4 and 5 and two of
  its forbids bullets; and ADR-0161's bullet *"Describing the lane loop as covered by tests, or reading a green suite
  as evidence about it."*, *"only once `s8e` §8e.11's `lane_e2e` tests pass"*. ADR-0109 decision 3 is **NOT
  superseded** (D21-42 8d).
- **ADR-0184**: *the `Gate:` value carries kind, reach and state, and its deadline is integer unix seconds*.
  **Supersedes in part** ADR-0105 clause 3's value delegation for `Gate:`, and design §5.4's `Gate:` row and §5's
  `gate_answer_void` row by dated lines. Its decisions 2 and 5 are D21-94's confirmation 2.
- **ADR-0185**: *`worktree_assigned`, `stage_empty` and `git_op`, three tier-2 kinds, minted by ADR-0116's route*.
  Supersedes nothing. Its decision 3 (`answer` refuses fail-closed on a deleted database) is D21-94's confirmation 3.

## D21-98 · ADR-0181 flips to `accepted` — the tier-1 writer is one method on `EventStore`, `append_tier1`, written git-first by the kernel

**Confirmed 2026-09-28 by the operator, in the session executing `s8e`, under D21-64 ruling 10.** ADR-0181 (`s8e`'s
record) flips `proposed → accepted`, `date: 2026-09-28` unchanged. It was written `proposed` at `s8e`'s build (package
S8E-P3) and revised in place during that stage while it bound nothing (D21-96, package D2196-8e; and dated notes from
packages S8E-P7, R1FIX-8e and R2FIX-8e). The ruling reads:
*"Standing authority for the rest of this session (`s8d` onward): each stage's own records flip at that stage's close
under the same two conditions, each with its own dated ledger line citing this entry. This lifts D21-49's flip-stops
for those records."* The two conditions are ruling 9's, *"if the formal Checkpoint 8c passes whole and a refuting
round finds no blocker or major"*, read for this stage's Checkpoint 8e.

Both conditions hold. The formal Checkpoint 8e passed whole at `492e516` (D21-97, its last paragraph: rc 0, 0 `FAIL`,
`CP8E-END-OF-BLOCK reached`, the extracted block 531 lines; (8)'s whole suite 1119 passed / 0 failed / 9 ignored over
44 result lines). Refuting round 4 (`s8e` §8e.13, item 29) was dry: no blocker, no major.

The text that flips is the one at `492e516`; the flip commit changes only its `status` line. No Decision clause and no
forbids bullet moves. The supersession in part of ADR-0158 decision 3 (*"No document, record or checkpoint may state
that AC-162 is met, that a tier-1 row is written, or that a failed git write leaves zero store rows"*) and its bullet
*"Claiming AC-160, AC-161, AC-162 or AC-163 is met, on any axis"*, and of ADR-0155 decision 5(b) (*"No document may
state that AC-162 is met for this axis, or for any."*) and its bullet *"Stating or implying that AC-162 is satisfied
for stage changes 2..N of a lane"*, only for the criteria `s8e` measures (§8e.14), which are all four, measured over
fixture repositories, is now in force; both stay `accepted`. **The flip commit writes the two `superseded_by`
back-links**, `0181` appended to ADR-0155's and ADR-0158's lists, with their README status cells and two
Supersessions-table rows. ADR-0003, ADR-0105 clauses 1, 2, 4, 5, 8 and 9, ADR-0158's `rk-cli` bullet (on D21-48 X10's
reading), ADR-0109 decision 2 and ADR-0118 clause 4 are cited and not superseded. D21-96 is the ruling it carries

## D21-105 · The AFK run over `s8f` … Step 8: flip authority extends, spend is S-BIN only under a US$30 cap, one-way doors are taken reversibly, and `s8g` weighs the `Findings` producer

**Taken 2026-09-28 by the operator, in the session that closed `s8e`, on four questions with options and a
recommendation, before an extended AFK session.**

**Ruled.**
1. **Flip authority extends.** D21-64 ruling 10's standing flip authority (*"for the rest of this session"*) carries
   into the AFK session for `s8f`, `s8g`, `s8h`, `s8i`, `s8j` and Step 8: each stage's own records flip at that
   stage's close under ruling 9's two conditions (the formal checkpoint passes whole, and a refuting round finds no
   blocker or major), each on its own dated ledger line citing this entry.
2. **Spend.** The ONLY spend authorised is `s8i`'s S-BIN dispatches against the real account, capped at **US$30
   total**, run in the main session in the foreground, with a spend table. Every other live or paid step (the live
   probes of `s8h` and `s8j`, `s8j`'s paid runs, Checkpoint 7c's paid re-run, any OpenRouter or Jev use) is prepared to
   its go-ahead line and deferred to the review packet.
3. **One-way doors.** A decision that is hard to reverse is taken in its most reversible form (a `proposed` record, no
   irreversible act), recorded TAKEN PROVISIONALLY, and the session continues; never anything destructive, remote or
   paid beyond item 2. Decisions that arise are taken by the weigh-in method and recorded both in the weigh-in ledger
   and as provisional D-entries, for the operator's review.
4. **The shipped `Findings` producer (D21-95 item 2)** is decided in `s8g` by the weigh-in method: measure both routes
   and take the recommendation provisionally.

## D21-106 · `s8f` §8f.2 item 11's STOP: `call_feed` gains one `store` arm, through one fail-closed seam shared with `item_create`

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-09-28, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-b59409** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to A).

**The STOP.** Measured at `37bba5c` by package S8F-P1-RECORDS: `Runtime::call_feed` (`crates/rk-kernel/src/runtime/step.rs`)
refuses every feed verb that is not a no-input `toolchain` row (*"a feed is produced by a `toolchain` row with no
inputs"*), and §8f.7's `backlog` verb is `route = "store"`. §8f.2 item 11: *"If the resolver has no path from a feed's
`verb` to a `store`-route handler, the producer has no call site. STOP: the resolver is `s8d`'s."* No accepted record
fixes feeds as toolchain-only (ADR-0170 decision 8 admits *"a registry verb"*).

**Ruled (blocker panel, three lensed judges and a synthesiser; A scored 8.5 / 8.5 / 8, B 2 / 2 / 2, C 3 / 4.5 / 4).**
1. `call_feed` admits exactly one new shape: `route == Store` with no declared inputs. Every other combination keeps
   the existing by-name refusal, byte-unchanged.
2. One composition-root-bound struct seam in `rk-kernel` serves both the step-grain `Route::Store` arm (`item_create`)
   and the new feed arm. `rk-kernel` still does not depend on `rk-store`.
3. The seam's kernel default **refuses by name** and never returns an empty value (unlike `UnwiredReadSeam`, whose
   `read` returns `Ok(())`): an unbound kernel that served an empty `BacklogSnapshot` would be N14's silent `null`.
4. The arm's result still passes `decode_verb_output` against the row's `outputs`.
5. The `backlog` verb row, both `[feed.*.backlog]` rows and the two tests that pin `backlog` as `unprovided`
   (`source_registry::the_catalog_source_census_is_fifty_with_fourteen_declared`,
   `reference_resolver::kb_checklist_and_feed_keys_resolve_through_the_declared_tables_and_an_undeclared_key_refuses_by_name`)
   move in one commit, each by a dated in-file note.
6. Tests carried by the package that builds the arm: the red-first
   `feed::closure_record_audit_resolves_toolchain_backlog_through_the_store_route`, an unbound-seam refusal, and a
   negative-route test (a git-route feed and a store-route feed with one input still refuse with the existing
   message).
7. ADR-0189 decision 12 (proposed) carries it, with one added sentence quoting ADR-0170's *"`store`: an rk-store
   write;"* and stating that a no-input store-route read is within that route (§8f.7: *"No route is added"*). No
   accepted ADR is superseded. A dated note goes in `s8d`'s file. Checkpoint 8d stays provisional and is re-run whole
   at Checkpoint 8f (9).

**Reversal:** delete the arm and the seam's feed use; `[feed.*.backlog]` returns to `unprovided` and `closure-record`
refuses at `audit` again (option C).

*(dated 2026-09-29, `s8f` build, package R2FIX-8f-B-RECORDS; refuting round 2; a correction NOTE, the ruling is not
rewritten.)* Ruling 2's *"`rk-kernel` still does not depend on `rk-store`"* is **inaccurate as a fact about the tree**:
`crates/rk-kernel/Cargo.toml` carries `rk-store = { path = "../rk-store" }` in `[dependencies]` (Slice 3's option A,
for `rk_store::canon`), and `crates/rk-kernel/src/runtime/reference.rs` holds `impl ArtifactReadSeam for
rk_store::render::ArtifactReader`. What the ruling fixes stands: ONE composition-root-bound struct seam serves both
store-route arms, and its kernel default refuses by name (ruling 3). The seam's ground is that **the kernel opens no
managed tree** (ADR-0189 decision 12's dated correction, `proposed`), not a missing crate edge. The same clause in
`s8f` §8f.2 item 10 takes a dated note, and the inaccuracy is stated for the review packet in §8f.5's list (reading 9).

## D21-107 · `s8f`: ADR-0190's carrier widens `ActivitySpec`, superseding ADR-0138 and ADR-0174 in part for one member

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-09-28, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-838696** (authority-bound: **left OPEN** in the weigh-in ledger for the operator; the work proceeds on it).

**The blocker.** ADR-0138 (accepted), first forbids bullet: *"Forbids adding a step list, or any sixth field, to
`rk_types::activity::ActivitySpec`."* ADR-0174 (accepted) d1 repeats *"`ActivitySpec` is not edited (ADR-0099's five
fields; ADR-0138's forbids)"* and d10 *"ADR-0138's decisions 1–5 and its forbids stand"*. §8f.8 settles the carrier
as *"a member on `ActivitySpec` that is outside the prompt and outside every digest"*, and §8f.3's telemetry row named
only ADR-0131 and ADR-0163 as superseded.

**Ruled (blocker panel; option A′, A widened).**
1. `ActivitySpec` gains `activity_id_at_version: Option<String>`, set explicitly at every construction site (no
   `Default`, no `..` spread), `Some("<id>@<version>")` from `bind_nodes` and each per-step spec, `None` in fixtures
   and fakes. `spec_json` stays its five-member object; `spec_from_json` repopulates the member from `RecordedSpec`.
   The member is kept out of the prompt, the card digest, the memo key and `run_id`. The adapter alone writes it on
   its own `dispatch_completed` row (ADR-0126 cited, untouched).
2. ADR-0190 (proposed) d9 is widened to a quoted supersession in part, for this one member and until ADR-0131's tag
   is wired, of: ADR-0138's first forbids bullet (*"or any sixth field"* only); ADR-0138 Decision 3's `ActivitySpec`
   half; ADR-0138 Decision 2's *"NOT a sixth spec field"* as applied to this id; ADR-0174 d1's parenthetical and d10's
   sentence. `0174` joins ADR-0190's `supersedes`. **ADR-0099 d1 is carried as a declared reading** (it names what
   the spec carries from `StepSpec`; it is not a closed list), **flagged for the operator's ruling**; if declined it
   converts to a supersession in part.
3. Engineering conditions on the §8f.8 package: a byte-identity test (`spec_json`, card digest, memo key and `run_id`
   identical with the member `Some` or `None`), a replay round-trip test, a producer-path test, mutants M16a/M16b
   (the member forced to `None` in `spec_from_json` and in `bind_nodes`), the two compile-fail `.stderr` files shown
   red then re-blessed if their lines move, and every code doc asserting *"five fields and no sixth"* amended to cite
   ADR-0190 (proposed).
4. `rk-types` moves, so **Checkpoint 5 is provisional** and is re-run whole inside Checkpoint 8f (D21-97 (10i)'s
   precedent).

**Fallback, if the operator declines:** option B, a kernel-side `EventStore` decorator that stamps the member onto the
adapter's rows, with its ADR-0126 tension recorded in a record. **ADR-0190 does not flip at `s8f`'s close while this
entry is unconfirmed** (it moves two accepted records' Decisions); it stays `proposed` under D21-49's flip-stop and
goes on the review packet's confirm list.

*(Confirmed 2026-09-29 by the operator at a weigh-in sitting during the AFK run: Weigh-In wi-838696 decided A, "widen ActivitySpec (ADR-0190 as built)". This lifts the entry's hold: ADR-0190 flips to `accepted` at `s8g`'s close under D21-105 item 1, on `s8f`'s two conditions (the formal Checkpoint 8f passed whole at `ae38553` with ADR-0190 held, and refuting round 8 was dry), with its four `superseded_by` back-links (0131, 0138, 0163, 0174) and Checkpoint 8f (2)'s `FLIP_PENDING` emptied in the same commit. ADR-0099 decision 1's declared reading is confirmed with it. The operator also confirmed Checkpoint 8f (2)'s `FLIP_PENDING` form at the same sitting: Weigh-In wi-f613b1 decided A.)*

## D21-108 · `s8f`: s8e's `lane_e2e` dirty arm is rewritten by S8F-P3 over a real `exclude` answer

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-09-28, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-205855** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to A).

**The blocker.** At `afb60ff` the whole suite reads 1130 passed / 1 failed / 9 ignored over 44; the one failure is the
dirty arm of `lane_e2e::a_closed_lane_removes_its_clean_landed_tree_and_keeps_a_dirty_one`, whose harness writes *"a
tracked file that nothing lands"*. D21-43 N10 and ADR-0188 decision 2 land that edit at the step's own commit, so the
tree is clean at the closed mark and ADR-0180's removal rule (accepted) correctly removes it.

**Ruled.** S8F-P3 owns the arm and rewrites it in the package that binds `Boundary::with_excluded` to the real
`landing.refused-path` gate, through `answer … exclude` (no test-visible seam). The harness writes a denylisted
untracked path on **every** dispatch (so the re-entry discard cannot erase it); invocation 1 stops at the gate;
`exclude` is answered; invocation 2 reaches `closed`; the arm asserts the excluded path is dirty in the run tree and
the tree still exists and is registered, with PREMISE asserts on the gate row, the answer and the closed mark. The
test name and Checkpoint 8e's `sel rk-cli 38` line stay byte-unchanged. P3 re-runs S8E-M37/M38 in a private worktree
and adds a mutant *"a landing stages excluded paths"*. **Until P3 lands, the arm stays red, not ignored**; if P3
cannot finish it, P3 stops as a named blocker (no `#[ignore]`, no edit to Checkpoint 8e). No accepted ADR moves.

## D21-109 · `s8f`: M24's lane arm is unobservable by construction; the rk-store test kills M24 and a construction lint pins the guarantee

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-09-28, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-8443fa** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to A).

**The blocker.** Measured by S8F-P4 in a private worktree after a 10/10 green control. M24 (`if self.entry == 0 ||
current != self.entry {` → `if false {` in `rk_store::render::ArtifactReader::serve`) is killed on
`render::a_read_before_the_entrys_reconcile_is_refused` and survives, rc 0, on
`landing::every_artifact_read_in_a_lane_follows_its_entrys_reconcile`. A reader is built only from the `Reconciled`
that `reconcile_entry` returns (private fields), and every invocation writes one `reconcile_started` before the pass,
so no lane can read before its entry's reconcile. §8f.12's M24 row named both tests as must-flip, and golden
constraint 12 blocks on a survivor.

**Ruled (blocker panel; option (a+)).**
1. `render::a_read_before_the_entrys_reconcile_is_refused` is M24's killing test. The landing test stays as AC-126's
   replay-ordering regression (red at entry with zero reads) and is no longer M24's must-flip. The survival is
   **explained**, not quarantined (s8c §8c.9: *"the guard moves; it is not weakened"*; precedents s8c M25, s8b M11).
2. A mandatory construction lint, `render::a_reconciled_is_built_only_by_the_entry_pass` (rk-store `tests/it/render.rs`,
   one new test, floor +1), scans CODE LINES only under `crates/*/src` with EXACT counts: the struct literal
   `Reconciled {` once, inside `reconcile_entry`; no `pub fn` returning `Self`/`Reconciled`, no `Default` or `From`
   impl, no `pub` field; rk-cli's `Option<Reconciled>` binding a function-local `= None` in the invocation loop, with no
   `static`/`OnceLock`/`LazyLock`/`thread_local!` holder. Each check has a planted-hit control. Mutant **M24-G** plants
   each of these in turn and must flip the lint.
3. **M24-C** (the entry pass taken after the invocation's first read, or the `Option<Reconciled>` carried across
   invocations) is an optional informational row and does not gate. Deleting the `reconcile_entry` call is NOT
   evidence about ordering: `stage_seams` then returns `Exit::ConfigError`, a proxy.
4. Option (b), a test seam or a `pub` constructor, is rejected: it is the *"configuration, cache or fast path that
   skips it"* AC-126 and ADR-0189 decision 9 forbid.
5. No record moves: ADR-0189 is `proposed` and its Decision is unchanged.

**Reversal:** delete the lint and M24-G, restore the landing test to M24's must-flip column, and take (b) or a measured
(c).

## D21-110 · `s8f`: ADR-0189 decision 9's reader root arm is deferred to `s8j`, and decision 9 is narrowed in-record before the flip

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-09-29, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-18a1cc** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to A).

**The blocker.** S8F-P8 found that ADR-0189 decision 9's last sentence (*"A vouched root has no `emit` binding, so the
reader serves its bytes and digest without that check."*) has no caller at `7168908`:
- `rk_kernel::runtime::reference::read_artifact_input` resolves an `artifact:` input only from `emit_binding_of(…)`.
  With no committed emit binding, it returns *"no committed `emit` binding of … is in the journal"* before any seam
  call.
- `ArtifactReader::serve` reads only `<Kind>.json`, while a root is stored as `<Kind>.root<ext>`.
- No shipped question carries an `attaches` option.

**Ruled** (blocker panel; option A as all three judges refined it: defer to a named owner, fail closed, and narrow
the record before it flips):
1. **No code at `s8f`.** The interim behaviour stands:
   - a mandatory `artifact:` input naming a root refuses by name;
   - an optional one resolves to `null`, per ADR-0173's `?` arm. This is a stated residual.
2. **The owner is `s8j` §8j.2.** It is the first stage that authors `attaches` options (`root.bug-report`,
   `root.intake-record`) whose lanes require them. `s8j` builds the resolver's root arm and the reader's root arm
   together, if a lane reads a root's **bytes** through an `artifact:` input.
   - The value is typed by the kind's declared source or schema, never by whether the bytes parse as JSON.
   - The widening of ADR-0173's `artifact:` row is carried by a record superseding ADR-0173 in part, written
     `proposed` there.
   - If `s8j`'s lanes need only a `requires` presence check, `s8j`'s close states that the arm stays owed to the
     first consumer of a root's bytes.
3. **ADR-0189**, while still `proposed`, takes a dated in-record correction to decision 9's last sentence naming this
   deferral and the interim behaviour. It may then flip at `s8f`'s close with the residual stated.
4. The dated notes go in `s8f` §8f.5, §8f.11 and its owed list, and in `s8j` §8j.2 as a forward pointer.

**Reversal:** build option C (end to end) or B (the `rk-store` half) at `s8f`, and strike the correction.

## D21-111 · `s8f`: `RK-D-UNREAD`'s read side is keyed by kind; any stage and any run consumes a write

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-09-29, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-93039a** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to D).

**The blocker.** S8F-P7 found that `RK-D-UNREAD` (`crates/rk-cli/src/doctor/rules.rs`, `fn unread`) keys both
`artifact.write` and `artifact.read` by each row's own `(stage, kind)`. As a result, a kind written at one stage and
read only at a later stage counts as unread at the writing stage. The fixture lane's `ImplReport`, written at
`implement` and read at `review`, is the case. The lane test
`doctor_journal::unread_counts_every_artifact_row_a_fake_harness_lane_writes` stays green only through a test-local
`check-report` activity.

**Ruled.** The blocker panel chose option D. One judge preferred A, the residual-only option, on reversibility.

1. **The read side.**
   - It becomes the set of `kind` over every `artifact.read` row, whatever its stage or run.
   - The write side is unchanged: the `(stage, kind)` key, `run_of` (null or absent means unknown), the distinct-run
     count, the window and `Warning` severity.
   - The filter is `!read.contains(&kind)`.
2. **`run` is never matched.** A `run_id` is `<stage>-<digest>` (ADR-0152), so a same-run join can never pair an
   implement write with a review read. That rules out options B and C.
3. **Item and journal-order matching are not added.** ADR-0116 d4 and AC-472 are kind-level. Both matches are a named
   residual owed to the operator. The precision cost is accepted: a kind read for one item silences writes of that kind
   for other items.
4. **Doc comments.** The one on `fn unread` and the one on `ArtifactRead::stage` in `runtime/reference.rs` are
   corrected.
5. **Tests and mutants.**
   - A red-first test over a 51-run cross-stage fixture: `ImplReport` is written at implement and read at review, and
     a control kind is never read. At N = 50 exactly one finding appears, and it is the control.
   - The lane test drops its workaround and asserts that `ImplReport` is not flagged.
   - The s7d 49/51 and write-set tests and the RK-D-UNREAD doctor fixture pass unedited.
   - Mutants M-a (the (stage, kind) read key restored), M-b (keyed on (kind, run)) and M-c (the read filter dropped),
     each killed in a private worktree.
6. **Records.**
   - No accepted Decision moves. The change conforms the rule to ADR-0116 decision 4 and ADR-0075 decision 5 (*"zero
     downstream reads"*).
   - The per-stage read key came from the RT-48 routing and a code doc comment, not from a Decision sentence.
   - ADR-0190's As-built sentence on cross-stage consumption is closed by a dated note citing this entry.

**Reversal:** revert the one-function change and the fixture, restore the lane test's workaround labelled as a proxy,
and strike this entry.

*(dated 2026-09-29, `s8f` build, package R2FIX-8f-B-RECORDS; refuting round 2; a correction NOTE, the ruling is not
rewritten.)* Item 6's *"The per-stage read key came from the RT-48 routing and a code doc comment, not from a Decision
sentence"* **omits the Decision text the key was read from.** C-01 **D-08** states the predicate as *"kind-per-stage"*:
DECISIONS-2026-09-18 D18-5 reads *"(the predicate C-01 D-08 fixes: kind-per-stage, the owner AC-426's wording, quoted
by ADR-0116)"*; AC-472's 2026-09-18 amendment reads *"The predicate is the owner's — kind-per-stage over the last N
runs — which ADR-0116 already quotes with N = 50."*; and `s7d` §7d.2 item 10 records D-08 as TAKEN with *"**kind-per-stage
over the last N runs, N = the repository's `unread_window_runs`**"*. RT-48 routed the `stage` member as *"the
per-stage discriminator D-08's kind-per-stage predicate needs"*. **This entry reads D-08's "kind-per-stage" as the
WRITE side** (a stage's produced kind, keyed `(stage, kind)` and counted over distinct runs, unchanged) **and D-08's
read side as kind-level**, which is AC-426's own THEN wording, *"WHEN a stage's produced artifact accumulates zero
downstream reads over that window"*: a downstream read is a read at any stage. That is a reading of D-08, not a
departure from it; it is stated for the operator with the rest of this provisional entry. No note is added to AC-472
or AC-426 here.

## D21-112 · `s8f`: after an `exclude` of a tracked path, `answer` derives the working state less the lane's excluded paths

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-09-29, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-81d7e4** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to A).

**The finding** (refuting round 3, major, confirmed by both refuters). `rk_git::landing::classify` never stages an excluded
tracked path. A gate opened by a later landed act binds the landed tree T (defect (b)'s re-mint). `answer`, however,
derives `TreeBasis::WorkingState`, which is `read-tree HEAD` plus `add -u` over every tracked modification, the excluded
one included. So the two trees differ and the first answer is void (AC-176). On a drift gate, a `move-to-prose`
answer is then dropped silently. The finder measured this in a private worktree at `bab968e`:
`T=1aa4829e… WorkingState=caec5880…`. §8f.4 says both *"`exclude` means every later landing of this lane leaves
exactly those paths unstaged"* and *"the answer's derivation matches the gate's"*, and neither restricts `exclude` to
untracked paths.

**Ruled (option A).**
1. `answer`'s tree derivation subtracts the lane's excluded paths, read exactly as `run` reads them
   (`tier_one::landing_answers`), so it equals the landed gate's tree.
2. It is covered by red-first lane tests: a tracked path is refused and answered `exclude`, and the next gate's answer
   is APPLIED, not void. The same holds for a drift gate answered `move-to-prose` under a tracked exclusion. A mutant
   drops the subtraction.
3. ADR-0188 (proposed) states the reading in decisions 6 and 7 by dated in-record note. No accepted Decision moves.

**Rejected:** option B, which makes `exclude` untracked-only and refuses it over a tracked path. It would leave a
tracked denylist false hit such as `id_map.rs` with no remedy, and the denylist's own doc names `exclude` as that
remedy. **Reversal:** take B, and revert the one derivation.

*(dated 2026-09-29, s8f build, package R4FIX-8f; refuting round 4's records minor.)* **Ruling 3's "No accepted
Decision moves" is corrected: one moves, in part.** ADR-0105 clause 4 (accepted) reads *"`git write-tree` on a
**temporary index** built from the current index plus tracked modifications"*, and `WorkingStateLess` leaves an
excluded tracked path's modification out. It is not a second derivation (one routine, the plain argv with no
exclusion, and gate open and answer read the same set), so the forbids bullet's *"Forbids a second `tree_hash`
derivation"* holds; the *"plus tracked modifications"* sentence does not, for an excluded path. ADR-0188
(proposed) now names it a **supersession of ADR-0105 clause 4 in part**, with the clause quoted in full, in
decision 6's R4FIX-8f note (decision 7 cites it). Its `supersedes:` line already names `0105`; ADR-0105's
`superseded_by` back-link moves at ADR-0188's flip. For the review packet: confirm D21-112 together with this
supersession. The option taken and the reversal are unchanged.

---

## D21-113 · `s8f`: the reader checks and serves the latest recorded write of the sidecar path; ADR-0189 supersedes ADR-0173 in part

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-09-29, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-61625f** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to A). Under D21-105's AFK rule 4, this moves an accepted Decision. It is
carried by a `proposed` record, ADR-0189, which flips at `s8f`'s close only if the operator has not objected in this
ledger. It is on the review packet's confirm list.

**The finding.** Refuting round 5 raised it as a major, and both refuters confirmed it.
- Reconcile accepting a prose edit (AC-152), or a drift gate answered `move-to-prose:<section>`, rewrites
  `<Kind>.json` through the writer, and nothing appends a new emit binding.
- So every later `artifact:<Kind>` read compares the sidecar's digest with the stale binding's and halts (Halt 4).
  It was measured in a private worktree at `2a5057d`: `Err(DigestMismatch { kind: "Spec" … })`.
- Even without the check, the resolver served `value.clone()` of the binding.
- It is reachable on shipped lanes: `adr-record.toml` reads `spec = "artifact:Spec"`.

**Ruled.** The blocker panel chose option (a′) unanimously (8 / 8 / 8).

1. **The emit binding stays the only presence and origin gate.** With no binding, a mandatory input refuses by name
   and a `?` input is `null`, with no seam call either way. Otherwise there is exactly one seam call per resolved
   input, and ADR-0173's count and single-emitter rule stand.
2. **`ArtifactReadSeam::read` returns the served value**, and the resolver serves that value, not the binding.
3. **`ArtifactReader::serve` checks against the latest `artifact.write`** whose item matches and whose `path` is the
   kind's sidecar, `<Kind>.json`.
   - It never selects by kind: a restore names `<Kind>.md`.
   - With no such row it falls back to the binding's canonical digest, which is byte-identical to today.
   - A digest that equals neither is still Halt 4.
   - This is the same LATEST-write rule `RK-D-ARTIFACT-MUTATION` applies (ADR-0189 decision 13).
4. **The replaying reader serves the blob at the recorded `tree`**, checked against the recorded digest, and appends
   nothing.
5. **No new row kind and no synthetic completion row.** Option (b) is rejected: completion rows feed five folds.
   Option (c), a residual, would leave a reachable Halt 4.
6. **Tests and mutants.**
   - T1: an accepted prose edit is served to a later stage, asserting the text.
   - T2: `move-to-prose`.
   - T3: an out-of-band sidecar edit still halts.
   - T4: replay.
   - Mutants M1 to M5 (kind-keyed lookup, serving the binding, dropping the check, replay serving the binding,
     always comparing with the binding), each killed.
7. **Records.**
   - ADR-0189 (proposed) gains `0173` in `supersedes`. Two quoted phrases of ADR-0173 are superseded in part: decision
     1's `artifact:` row, *"the committed `emit` binding of the activation that produced `Kind` for this item, read
     from the journal"*, and decision 8's *"serves the journalled binding"*. Only where the value and the digest come
     from moves.
   - ADR-0189's title's "Supersedes nothing" takes a dated correction, and decision 9 a dated note.
   - ADR-0173 stays `accepted`, and its `superseded_by` gains `0189` at 0189's flip.

**Reversal.** Revert the read path, retract the supersession, and take option (c).

*(dated 2026-09-29, s8f build, package R5FIX-8f.)* **Built in part; step 3 is returned to the judge panel as a
blocker.** Steps 1, 2, 4 and 5 are built, with the `rk-kernel` resolver test, T3 and T4 red then green and M2, M3 and
M4 killed; the records of ruling 7 are written in ADR-0189 (`proposed`). Step 3, the live arm's latest-write check,
is not built. The entry pass writes the accepted value into the run-owned worktree, and the reader reads
`rk-run/<item>`; the write lands only at the stage's first committed step, which comes after that step's read.
Measured in a private worktree with step 3 applied: every invocation of T1's lane exits `Unsupported` with no
dispatch, re-entry discards the unlanded `Spec.json` (*"not landed at a committed step"*), the next entry accepts
the edit again, and the run ref's sidecar never moves. T1 and T2 are written, red, and `#[ignore]`d; M1 and M5 are
owed with step 3. `s8f` §8f.5's R5FIX-8f note carries the measurement.

---

## D21-114 · `s8f`: amends D21-113 step 3 only; the entry pass's own writes land at entry, so the latest-write check cannot livelock

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-09-29, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-eda716** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to B).

**The blocker.** Package R5FIX-8f found that D21-113 step 3 livelocks when built as ruled. It measured this in a
private worktree: invocations 2, 3 and 4 each exit `Unsupported` with 0 dispatches, and the `Spec.json` writes go
2 → 3 → 4.
- The entry pass writes into the run-owned worktree: an accepted prose edit, an applied `move-to-prose`, or a stored
  vouched root.
- These writes land only at the stage's first committed step, and that step's read comes first.
- The reader reads `rk-run/<item>` (ADR-0189 decision 9), so the latest recorded write never matches the ref.
- The re-entry discard throws the write away, and the next entry writes it again.

**Ruled** (blocker panel, option B hardened as B′, 2–1; the engineering lens preferred A-tightened).

1. **The kernel.** `tier_one` gains `Content::Entry`, a trailerless content commit with the subject `rk entry <item>
   <stage> <run_id>` and no body, through `commit_act`.
   - It stages with the journalled baseline, the lane's exclusions and the denylist, size and path checks.
   - It appends only its existing `git_op` rows. There is no new row kind, no change to a row's shape, and no
     completion-shaped row, so D21-113 step 5 holds.
   - It makes zero git writes on replay.
   - `heal`, `step_checkpoints` and `marked_commits` ignore it by construction: its subject has no trailer and no
     `rk step ` prefix.
2. **The composition root.** The entry landing runs after the re-entry discard, `move_to_prose`, `reconcile_entry`
   and the vouched pass, and before any seam is handed to the kernel. It runs only when the entry pass wrote something
   and no drift gate's `Gate:` commit landed it. A refusal opens the `landing.refused-path` gate over an empty commit.
3. **The reader.** D21-113 step 3 is built exactly as ruled.
4. **Tests.** T1 and T2 are un-ignored as written. They are joined by:
   - `landing::an_accepted_prose_edit_lands_at_entry_before_the_first_read`
   - `landing::an_entry_landing_is_ignored_by_heal_and_the_tier1_folds`
   - `landing::an_entry_that_writes_nothing_makes_no_entry_act`
   - `landing::a_replayed_entry_landing_makes_no_git_write`

   Mutants M-ENTRY-LAND, M-ENTRY-HEAL and M-ENTRY-GATE are added, plus D21-113's owed M1 and M5.
5. **The records.** ADR-0188 (proposed) takes a dated in-record note on decision 2 naming this third landing point,
   and on decision 3's commit forms. No accepted Decision moves.

**Reversal.** Delete the one call site and the Entry arm, and take A-tightened: the run-ref blob is accepted when its
digest is among the recorded writes of the exact path at or after the emit's origin write, with the same-entry stale
read as a named residual. Commits already made are content-identical to what the next committed step would have
landed.

*(dated 2026-09-29, s8f build, package R5FIX2-8f.)* **Built** (the ruling's list names the package R6FIX-8f). The
kernel arm (`Content::Entry`, `EntryCheckpoint`, `ENTRY_SUBJECT_PREFIX`, `Act::entry`, the widened trailer-less guard
and the entry message), the composition root's call site (`run::land_entry`, on the `else` arm of the drift gate's
opening, only when the entry pass wrote) and D21-113 step 3 (the live arm's latest-write check) are in the tree.
Red first in the ruled order; T1 and T2 un-ignored; four named tests and a fifth (a drift entry lands once, through
its gate: the ruling's *"new assertion that exactly one content commit follows a drift entry"*); M-ENTRY-LAND,
M-ENTRY-HEAL, M-ENTRY-GATE, D21-113's owed M1 and M5 and a control mutant M-ENTRY-ALWAYS all killed in a private
worktree. Two existing tests asserted the superseded binding-only halt and take dated notes (`s8f` §8f.5). ADR-0188
decisions 2, 3 and 14 and ADR-0189 decision 9 carry dated in-record notes (both `proposed`). Checkpoint 8f (4)
selects 64 in `rk-cli`, and (5)'s floor is 1119 + 116 = 1235.

*(dated 2026-09-29, s8f build, package R7FIX-8f; refuting round 7's records minor.)* **Two attributions in the built
note above are corrected; the ruling is not rewritten.** (a) *"(the ruling's list names the package R6FIX-8f)"*: the
Ruled section above (items 1 to 5 and the Reversal) names no package, and the panel draft this entry was written from
names none either; the attribution is struck in effect, and its source is unrecorded. (b) *"the ruling's *"new
assertion that exactly one content commit follows a drift entry"*"*: that phrase is not in the ruling, whose item 4
lists four tests and three mutants and says nothing of a drift entry. It was the builder's wording, not a quotation.
The fifth test, `landing::a_drift_entry_lands_its_accepted_write_once_through_its_gate`, is the builder's addition
beyond the ruling's four, taken because no drift-gate test drove an entry that both drifts and writes. Nothing
selected, counted or floored moves. `s8f` §8f.12 carries the same correction.

## D21-115 · `s8f`: `exclude` refuses a path under the item's managed artifact directory; `abort` is the remedy

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-09-29, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-968637** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to B).

**The residual.** R7FIX-8f measured it with a temporary probe:
- A refused drift-gate landing names `.redkiln/artifacts/<item>/Spec.json` and `Spec.md`, and it is answered
  `exclude`.
- Every later landing therefore leaves the restore unstaged, as ADR-0188 decision 6 says.
- The ref keeps the human's structured edit, so each entry pass surfaces the drift again.
- Answering `confirm-restore` never releases the stage exit. Invocations 4 and 5 each exit 0 at a new
  `render.structured-drift` gate, and the lane stays in `review`.

This is a silent livelock. ADR-0188 decision 6, ADR-0189 decision 4 and D21-114 do not settle it.

**Ruled (option B).**
1. `answer … exclude` refuses, by name, a gate whose refused paths include a path under
   `.redkiln/artifacts/<item>/`. It appends nothing. Such a gate is answered `abort`, or the operator commits a fix on
   the run ref first. Other paths stay excludable.
2. This is covered by a red-first lane test and a mutant that drops the refusal.
3. ADR-0188 (proposed) states the rule in decision 6 by dated in-record note. No accepted Decision moves.

**Rejected.**
- Option A, a documented residual only, would leave a silent exit-0 livelock.
- Option C, not re-gating an answered drift digest, risks hiding a real drift.

**Reversal.** Remove the check, and take A or C.

---

## D21-116 · `s8f` closes: a run lands its work as content commits on its run ref, `rk-store` renders, writes and reads every managed artifact with structured drift gated, and `doctor` reads the store

**Taken 2026-09-29 by the session executing `s8f`, at the stage's close, under the operator's standing AFK rules
(D21-105).** It records and cites. It re-takes no ruling and writes no go-ahead line. Of the stage's three records,
ADR-0188 and ADR-0189 flip under D21-105 item 1, which carries D21-64 ruling 10 into the AFK session, each by its own
dated ledger line (D21-117, D21-118), not by this entry. ADR-0190 does not flip (D21-107, below). What this entry
records is what `s8f`'s §8f.3, §8f.4 … §8f.12 and §8f.13 record; every figure below is quoted from the stage file's
recorded readings, the tree or the committed records, except the formal reading in the last paragraph, which is
quoted from the run's output.

Records: the landing record = ADR-0188, the render record = ADR-0189, the telemetry-key record = ADR-0190 (§8f.3's
dated note, package S8F-P1-RECORDS: *"The records took, in the order written"*).

**Recorded — the three records, and how they flip.** Each id was recorded at write time in Checkpoint 8f (2)'s `REC_`
lines (D21-47: *"Records are located by the id recorded at write time"*); each record is `status: proposed` at
`ae38553`, in ADR-0085's form with its `### Option — ` headings and **What this forbids** block. The README's
next free number reads **`0191`**.
- **ADR-0188** (the landing record). **Supersedes in part** ADR-0105 clause 5 (*"Anything not listed is a
  successor"*) and its bullet *"Forbids a wrapper write not on clause 5's list"*, for three writes on the run-owned
  worktree: `commit-tree`, `update-ref refs/heads/rk-run/<item>` and `read-tree HEAD` on its own index. Since
  R4FIX-8f it also supersedes ADR-0105 clause 4 in part (D21-112's correction note: *"ADR-0188 (proposed) now names
  it a **supersession of ADR-0105 clause 4 in part**"*), for one case: once the lane's journal records an `exclude`,
  the no-checkpoint-due derivation is *"the current index plus tracked modifications"* less the lane's excluded
  paths. Amended before acceptance by dated in-record notes under D21-112, D21-114 (the entry landing, a third landing
  point) and D21-115 (`exclude` refuses a managed artifact path), and by refuting rounds 1 to 7's record fixes. Its
  title names clause 5 only (*"Supersedes `0105` clause 5 in part, for three writes"*); D21-117's flip commit adds a
  dated title correction naming clause 4, and moves nothing else in the record's text.
- **ADR-0189** (the render record). **Supersedes in part** ADR-0173 decision 1's `artifact:` row, whose "Resolves
  to" cell reads *"the committed `emit` binding of the activation that produced `Kind` for this item, read from the
  journal"*, and decision 8's *"serves the journalled binding"*, only where the served value and the checked digest
  come from (D21-113, as D21-114 amends its step 3). Its decision 12 carries D21-106, decision 9 is narrowed in-record
  by D21-110 (*"ADR-0189, while still `proposed`, takes a dated in-record correction to decision 9's last sentence
  naming this deferral and the interim behaviour. It may then flip at `s8f`'s close with the residual stated."*), and
  its title's "Supersedes nothing" took a dated correction while `proposed` (R5FIX-8f).
- **ADR-0190** (the telemetry-key record). **Supersedes in part** ADR-0131's first forbids bullet and four passages of
  ADR-0163, for `dispatch_completed.activity_id_at_version`, and, under D21-107 item 2, ADR-0138 and ADR-0174 for the
  same one member (*"`0174` joins ADR-0190's `supersedes`"*). **It is held `proposed`.** D21-107: *"ADR-0190 does not
  flip at `s8f`'s close while this entry is unconfirmed (it moves two accepted records' Decisions); it stays `proposed`
  under D21-49's flip-stop and goes on the review packet's confirm list."* Weigh-In `wi-838696` is open. Its four
  back-links, on `0131`, `0138`, `0163` and `0174`, do not move.
- **The back-links were not written with the records** (§8f.3: *"The `superseded_by` back-links move in each record's
  flip commit"*). D21-117's flip commit appends `0188` to ADR-0105's list and D21-118's appends `0189` to ADR-0173's,
  with their README status cells and two Supersessions-table rows.
- **Checkpoint 8f (2) and the flip-stop.** The faithful formal run at `2acb35a` read rc 1 at (2) on the three
  `proposed` records: each record's *"not accepted"* and *"no dated DECISIONS heading"* lines and the six back-link

- **Two records flip at this close under D21-105 item 1:** *"each stage's own records flip at that stage's close under
  ruling 9's two conditions (the formal checkpoint passes whole, and a refuting round finds no blocker or major), each
  on its own dated ledger line citing this entry."* Refuting round 8 was dry (below); the formal reading is the last
  paragraph of this entry. One dated line per record follows the ruling's letter.

**Recorded — the provisional rulings this stage carries.** Each was TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105)
pending the operator's confirmation, recorded in the weigh-in ledger, and is on the review packet's confirm list.
- **D21-106** (`wi-b59409`, defaulted A): `call_feed` gains one `store` arm through one fail-closed seam shared with
  `item_create`; ADR-0189 decision 12 carries it. Its ruling 2's *"`rk-kernel` still does not depend on `rk-store`"*

  operator. ADR-0190's flip waits on it.
- **D21-108** (`wi-205855`, defaulted A): s8e's `lane_e2e` dirty arm is rewritten over a real `exclude` answer.
- **D21-109** (`wi-8443fa`, defaulted A): M24's lane arm is unobservable by construction; the `rk-store` test kills
