- **D21-110** (`wi-18a1cc`, defaulted A): ADR-0189 decision 9's reader root arm is deferred to `s8j`, and decision 9
  is narrowed in-record before the flip.
- **D21-111** (`wi-93039a`, defaulted D): `RK-D-UNREAD`'s read side is keyed by kind; item and journal-order matching
  stay a named residual owed to the operator.
- **D21-112** (`wi-81d7e4`, defaulted A): after an `exclude` of a tracked path, `answer` derives the working state
  less the lane's excluded paths. Its R4FIX-8f note: *"For the review packet: confirm D21-112 together with this
  supersession."* ADR-0188's clause-4 supersession rests on it.
- **D21-113** (`wi-61625f`, defaulted A): the reader checks and serves the latest recorded write of the sidecar path;
  ADR-0189 supersedes ADR-0173 in part. It is carried by ADR-0189, *"which flips at `s8f`'s close only if the operator
  has not objected in this ledger"*: no entry follows D21-115 before this close, and none of D21-105 … D21-115 records
  an objection.
- **D21-114** (`wi-eda716`, defaulted B, 2–1): the entry pass's own writes land at entry as a trailerless `rk entry`
  content commit, so the latest-write check cannot livelock. The engineering lens dissented for A-tightened.
- **D21-115** (`wi-968637`, defaulted B): `exclude` refuses a path under the item's managed artifact directory, and
  `abort` is the remedy.

**Recorded — what was built** (`afb60ff` S8F-P1/P2, `00c2a7a` S8F-P3/P4, `0cb8ebc` S8F-P5/P6, `7168908` S8F-P7/P8
and the whole pass, `c0fb7db` … `518c74f` the round fixes, `c4e0078` and `b0e288a` D21-113 and D21-114, `d2cb092`
D21-115, `2acb35a` round 8's minors, `ae38553` (2)'s flip-stop line; the ledger lines `91e7b0f`, `9a0e39b`,
`debde0a`, `84c2786`, `153e9d6`, `d61d152` and `7e7acd7`).
- **The landing (ADR-0188, §8f.4).** `rk-git`'s primitive is `crates/rk-git/src/landing.rs`: one classification
  function, `landing::classify`, which the landing and the re-entry discard both call; the staging set is checked
  against the denylist, the 2 MiB blob limit and `0180`'s worst-case path bound before `commit-tree` and `update-ref`
  on a per-item temporary index. `tier_one`'s `Content` gains the landed and step arms, so every marked act lands and a
  step act commits only when the step changed the tree. The heal's step-checkpoint arm serves a landed step, never
  re-dispatching it over its own edit (finding 11). A refused landing opens the `landing.refused-path` gate (`exclude`
  | `abort`) over the no-checkpoint-due derivation, and an applied `exclude` binds every later boundary of the lane.
  Defect (b) is closed: `prepare_inputs` hashes the run-owned tree, and a gate opened by a landing act binds T. The
  entry landing (D21-114) lands the entry pass's own writes before the first read, and `exclude` over a managed
  artifact path is refused by name (D21-115). Nothing is integrated; `explain` prints the hand-off.
- **The render, write and read path (ADR-0189, §8f.5, §8f.6).** Layout C from a canonical JSON sidecar that is the
  typed value; one `artifact.write` per write and one `artifact.read` per read, the reader the sole emitter of
  `artifact.read` and refused before the entry's reconcile; the resolver serves what the reader served, checked
  against the latest recorded write of the sidecar path (D21-113).
- **C2, surfaced, restored and gated (§8f.5).** One `structured_drift` per structured edit, a restore, and a
  `render.structured-drift` gate that holds the stage's exit until it is answered `confirm-restore` or
  `move-to-prose:<section>`; the stage advance refuses by name while it is unanswered.
- **The vouched root (§8f.5).** `rk_store::vouched` stores an attached root at the next entry, and a change to it is
  one advisory drift under ADR-0025, never restored, rewritten or gated.
- **`item_create` and the `backlog` feed (§8f.7; D21-106).** `rk_store::item_write` writes `.redkiln/items/<id>.toml`
  in the run-owned worktree; `rk_store::backlog` reads each named item record by its exact path; both `[feed.*.backlog]`
  rows read `verb = "backlog"`, served through one seam that refuses by name when unbound.
- **The telemetry key, `cost` and `status` (ADR-0190, §8f.8; D21-107).**
  `ActivitySpec.activity_id_at_version: Option<String>`, carried outside the prompt and every digest onto
  `dispatch_completed`; `cost`'s per-activity breakdown and the one draw renderer `status` shares.
- **`doctor` over the store (§8f.9).** `doctor` reads the store's journal (`JournalSource::Store`), the
  flat-reading silencing of `RK-D-HEADROOM-MARGIN` is fixed, and the rule table moves *"from **39 ids to 41**"*:
  `RK-D-ARTIFACT-MUTATION` (AC-159) and `RK-D-DRIFT-KIND` (AC-127). `RK-D-UNREAD`'s `run_of` reads a null run as no
  run (D21-48 X11), and its read side is keyed by kind (D21-111).
- **The notes §8f.10 owes** are written (the AC register's AC-245, AC-473, AC-521, AC-478, AC-125, AC-196, AC-197 and
  AC-372 notes, §0.3's row C2 marked resolved, design §5.4's rows, and the `s8c`, `s8d`, `s7i`, `s7d` and `s7g` notes).

## D21-119 · `s8g`: the shipped `Findings` producer: both readers key on the review verdict kind, and the seal reads only a review activation's row

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105, items 3 and 4) of 2026-09-29, pending the operator's
confirmation — Weigh-In wi-e8a53d** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to A). This is the decision D21-105 item 4 assigns
to `s8g`: *"measure both routes … and take the recommendation provisionally"*. The ruling moves accepted ADR-0186 in
part, so a `proposed` record carries it. Under D21-105 AFK rule 4 that record flips at `s8g`'s close only if the
operator has not objected in this ledger. The ruling is on the review packet's confirm list.

**Measured by package S8G-P1** (at `178868f`, code read). Four findings:

1. **No shipped activity provides `Findings`.** None of the 102 shipped activities does. The review stage's verdict
   producers (adversarial-review, checklist-review, code-review, formal-inspection and spike-decision-review) provide
   `["ReviewVerdict"]`.
2. **The lanes select adversarial-review, not code-review.** Both lanes do. Code-review's `max_rigor` keeps it out of
   a mandatory review stage, so D21-95's first route (*"`code-review` provides `Findings`"*) would not reach the seal.
3. **The entry check is inert.** `findings_stage_reached` tests `provides.first() == "Findings"`.
4. **The seal is not inert.** `review_pass::findings_in` takes the last committed row whose step schema is `Findings`,
   from any activation. About 40 shipped agent steps outside the review stage emit `value:Findings`, among them
   fix-spec/adversary and root-cause/adversary. So a non-review adversary can trigger the review seal or displace the
   review verdict. ADR-0186's *"both the entry check and the seal are inert"* is false on its seal half.

**Ruled** (blocker panel, option 1, unanimous: 8.5 / 8 / 8.5). This is D21-95 item 2's second route: *"the pass keys on
what the shipped review provides"*.

1. **One shared predicate.** `review_pass::REVIEW_VERDICT_KIND = "ReviewVerdict"` and `is_review_activity`
   (*provides first* is that kind) are matched by exact element equality, never by substring. security-review's
   `SecurityReviewVerdict` is the near miss. Both readers call it, so they cannot diverge.
2. **The seal side.** A candidate row is a committed row whose schema is `Findings` and whose
   `activity_id_at_version` resolves, against the catalog loaded in the same invocation, to a review activity. The seal
   takes the last such row. An unresolvable id is refused by name.
3. **The entry side.** `findings_stage_reached` keys on the same predicate, so the entry check becomes live.
4. **Two commits.** The first scopes the seal; it narrows the rows and fails closed. The second makes the entry check
   live. Before the second, the full suite and the replayed guards run. A fixture that starts refusing for lack of a
   slice gets a declared slice; the check is never weakened.
5. **Tests.** Seven red-first tests, (a) to (g), cover:
   - (a) the entry refusal;
   - (b) review seals, approved and changes-requested;
   - (c) a fix-spec/adversary row does not seal;
   - (d) a post-review adversary does not displace the review verdict;
   - (e) security-review and design-review do not seal;
   - (f) an unresolvable id is refused;
   - (g) formal-inspection's two Findings steps.

   Mutants M1 to M5 are each killed.
6. **Records.**
   - A new `proposed` ADR (next free id) supersedes in part only ADR-0186's *"whose `provides` first is `Findings`"*
     sentences, *"The key is the one `review_pass` selects its committed activation by"*, and the inert-seal gap
     statement.
   - ADR-0162's decisions 3 and 5 stand whole, and decision 5 is now enforced.
   - D21-95 item 1's *"provides first is `Findings`"* is read as *the review verdict kind*.
   - No catalog version, CHANGELOG, `artifacts.toml`, lattice or Checkpoint 8b pin moves.

**Rejected.**
- Route one, retargeted to adversarial-review, means catalog churn and still mis-seals without this scoping.
- A one-constant move of `FINDINGS_SCHEMA` to `ReviewVerdict` is non-viable: no committed step row carries that schema.
- Deferring leaves a live mis-seal hazard.

**Reversal.** Revert the two commits and the proposed record.

---

## D21-120 · `s8g`: a pass-sourced kind holds its stage's advance: `replay::resume` refuses by name until the pass's `artifact.write` is journalled

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-09-29, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-548450** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to A).

**The blocker.** Package S8G-P3 found that ADR-0191 decision 1 (*"stage completeness still requires its
`artifact.write`"*) is enforced nowhere.
- `fsm::next_action` returns `AdvanceStage` once the plan is exhausted.
- A pass-sourced instance has no plan node.
- `.redkiln/artifacts.toml` already reads `[artifact.TestReport] source = "pass"` (P4).

So every verify stage would advance with no `TestReport` written.

**Ruled.** The panel's three judges converged on a guard inside `rk_kernel::replay::resume`, a fourth option none of
the three listed options described.
1. **Where it runs.** The check runs in `resume`'s `AdvanceStage` arm, before `rt.step`. It also runs in its `Done`
   arm when there is no successor, so a held lane end is not an end.
2. **What is owed.** The owed set is `selection.pass_sourced()` minus every instance that has a journalled
   `artifact.write` for this item at this stage. A row matches on its kind plus its toolchain key element (ADR-0073).
   Only rows after the stage's latest entry count. The check never uses Classification alone: rk-cli seeds no
   `ArtifactState`, so a Classification-only guard would refuse for ever.
3. **The refusal.** While anything is owed, `advance_refused` names the class and every owed instance (for example
   `pass-owed: TestReport{rust}`). `RecordedRun` gains a typed `pass_owed()`, and the loop breaks. There is no step,
   no commit, no mark and no row; this is the refusal shape of ADR-0189 decision 4.
4. **rk-cli.** In the verify-pass package, rk-cli runs `verify_pass` for each owed instance and calls `resume` again
   for the same stage. It retries once and does not push the stage onto `visited` a second time. If the pass does not
   complete, rk-cli exits held, naming the refusal.
5. **Tests.** Three red-first tests, in which the guard:
   - refuses with no write;
   - advances once a matching write exists;
   - holds the lane end.

   Mutants: the check removed, the journal filter dropped, the lane-end arm dropped.
6. **Records.** `fsm::next_action`, the `NextAction` set, `Runtime::step`, `module_boundary` and Checkpoint 7b are
   unchanged. ADR-0191 (proposed) takes a dated in-record note naming where decision 1's completeness clause is
   enforced. No accepted Decision moves: ADR-0152 decision 2 holds, because the arm still breaks.

**Reversal.** Delete the guard and the rk-cli branch.

---

## D21-121 · `s8g`: `AuthoredTests` is keyed by toolchain; ADR-0191 supersedes ADR-0132's keyed-set enumeration in part

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-09-29, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-9b529f** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to A). Under D21-105 AFK rule 4, the ruling moves an accepted Decision, and
the proposed ADR-0191 carries it. ADR-0191 flips at `s8g`'s close only if the operator has not objected in this
ledger. The ruling is on the review packet's confirm list.

**The blocker.** Package S8G-P4 found the problem. ADR-0191 decision 4 reads *"it is not `keyed_by`"*. After N23,
`unit-tests` and `unit-tests-ue` both provide `AuthoredTests` at implement, both at `method_rank` 20. Left unkeyed,
every shipped selection refuses to load:
`Load(RankTie { kind: "AuthoredTests", … activities: ["unit-tests", "unit-tests-ue"] })`. While the pair provided the
keyed `TestReport`, ADR-0073's per-instance expansion kept them from contending.

**Ruled.** The blocker panel chose option (a′).
1. **The pack.** `keyed_by = "toolchain"` stays on `[artifact.AuthoredTests]`, as P4 built it, with its PROVISIONAL
   comment until the operator rules. `spikes/p9/artifacts.toml` is not edited (ADR-0132 forbids it). No
   `method_rank`, `applicable_if`, tag or activity version moves.
2. **The record.** ADR-0191 (proposed) takes a dated in-record amendment.
   - It strikes decision 4's *"it is not `keyed_by`"*.
   - It adds `0132` to its `supersedes`, in part and at one site: ADR-0132's Decision enumeration, *"The
     toolchain-keyed set is **six**"*, and *"moves 5 → 6 keyed kinds"*. With `AuthoredTests` both become seven.
   - Every other clause of ADR-0132 and ADR-0073 stands verbatim, including the method_rank forbids bullet.
   - The reason is ADR-0132's own finding: *"The tie is a shadow of a kind that *is* keyed"*.

   ADR-0132's `superseded_by` gains `0191` at ADR-0191's flip.

**Rejected.**
- A catalog tie-break, such as a distinct rank for `unit-tests-ue`, re-opens F35 §1.3's defect: a mixed Node+UE item
  would author only one toolchain's tests.
- Not re-homing `unit-tests-ue` contradicts N23 and fails Checkpoint 8g (7d).

**Reversal.** Remove the keying line, revert the record amendment, and take the tie-break.

## D21-122 · `s8g`: the keyed `TestReport` write carries a `key` member and refuses a second key by name; ADR-0191 supersedes ADR-0189 decision 8's member list in part

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-09-29, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-85edf4** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to A). The ruling moves an accepted Decision, so a `proposed` record,
ADR-0191, carries it. Under D21-105 AFK rule 4, that record flips at `s8g`'s close only if the operator has not
objected in this ledger. The ruling is on the review packet's confirm list.

**The blocker.** Package S8G-P5 raised it.
- ADR-0189 (accepted) decision 8 lists the `artifact.write` row *"with these members"*: kind, item, stage, run,
  schema_version, digest, path. None of them is a key.
- D21-120 matches a keyed instance on its key.
- P5 built `rk_store::render::ArtifactWriter::write_keyed` provisionally. It writes the same `<Kind>.json` sidecar and
  one row with an added `key` member.

The residual as built: on a two-toolchain item, the second keyed write replaces the first key's sidecar while both rows
stand. D21-120's guard is then satisfied for a key whose bytes are gone, which is the silent stand-in ADR-0073
forbids.

**Ruled** (blocker panel, option 1′, unanimous):
1. **Keep the carrier.** Only `write_keyed` writes the `key` member. `write` is unchanged, and an unkeyed row has no
   `key`.
2. **Refuse a second key.** `write_keyed` refuses, by name and before any write, when the stage run already holds a
   keyed `artifact.write` of the same kind with a different `key`. The refusal is
   `RenderError::KeyedSidecarCollision { kind, prior_key, key }`, for example *"keyed-sidecar-collision:
   TestReport{node} after TestReport{rust}"*. Nothing is written and no row is appended.
   - The verify pass maps it to `Refused::Write`, so a two-toolchain item holds its stage by name and the guard keeps
     owing the second key.
   - A later write with the same key still replaces the sidecar, as decision 3 reads.
   - `served`'s key-blind replay match is unchanged. At most one key per kind per stage run can now exist, and its doc
     comment says so.
3. **Test it red first.** Two `write_keyed` calls with different keys: the second is refused, only one row exists, and
   the first sidecar is intact. A mutant drops the check.
4. **Records.** ADR-0191 (proposed) adds `0189` to its `supersedes`, in part, for decision 8's member list only (one
   additive member on keyed rows). Decision 3 stands. ADR-0189's `superseded_by` gains `0191` at ADR-0191's flip.

**Unreachable on shipped lanes today.** rk-cli sets no item toolchain set (0 `with_toolchain(` call sites), so
`TestReport` expands unkeyed there.

**Reversal.** Delete the collision check and `write_keyed`, and take option 4: refuse keyed owed instances until a
record decides per-key sidecars.

## D21-123 · `s8g`: mutation feasibility ships as a `mutation` declaration row in `[toolchain.rust]`, read by step 6 beside the pack's `[mutation]`

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-09-29, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-d4a46f** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to A).

**The gap.** Package S8G-P6 reported it. ADR-0192 (proposed) decision 2 reads *"The mutation declaration's shipped home
is the Rust row of ADR-0170's per-toolchain table, with `feasible = true`"*. Nothing ships that row, and neither shipped
pack declares `[mutation]`. Step 6 reads the pack's `[mutation]` table. So on the shipped packs, a lane whose `Spec`
carries criteria would be refused at verify by name (AC-337).

**Ruled** (blocker panel, option 1 tightened, as all three judges agreed):
1. **The registry row.** One declaration row goes in `[toolchain.rust]` of `.redkiln/registry.toml`, in the only shape
   the loader admits (`{value, source}`):
   `mutation = { value = { feasible = true }, source = "ADR-0104 decision 1 …; ADR-0192 decision 2; D21-42 4a" }`.
   It carries no `reason`. A dated comment cites ADR-0192 decision 2 and ADR-0170 decision 9: *"Adding a row is a
   record's act"*.
2. **No Node row.** A declaration key cannot carry `unmeasured`, and a value would be a guess. A dated comment says the
   row is owed with the first Node self-hosting run. Until then a Node lane with criteria stays refused by name.
3. **Admission.** `memo::DECLARATION_ROWS` gains `"mutation"` (3 → 4). The `verb_registry` pin and Checkpoint 8c's
   enumeration each admit it by a dated line.
4. **The reader.** Step 6 reads the registry row through the adapter the pass ran under (`toolchain_of`), the same way
   set-up reads `setup_projects`, so the kernel never sees `rust`. It keeps the pack's `[mutation]` table as a second
   reader.
   - Registry only, pack only, or both equal: that value is used.
   - Both present but different: refused by name (`Exit::ConfigError`).
5. **Tests.** Red-first tests cover:
   - a shipped Rust lane with criteria, not refused for feasibility;
   - the disagreement refusal;
   - a Node lane with criteria, refused by name.

   Mutants cover the registry read dropped and the disagreement check dropped.
6. **Records.** No accepted Decision moves: ADR-0170 decision 9 admits new declaration rows by record. ADR-0192
   (proposed) decision 2 is carried out as written.

**Reversal.** Remove the row and the registry reader, and amend ADR-0192 decision 2 to name the pack's table (option 2).

---

## D21-124 · `s8g` B1: the checklist envelope is unwrapped at the port by a fail-closed decorator in rk-cli's `real_toolchain`

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-09-29, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-9afdd0** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to A). No accepted Decision moves. The panel's full ruling is in the
run's judge-panel output (s8g package S8G-P7's items B1–B4), summarised here.

**The blocker.** S8G-P7 measured it. ADR-0173's resolver hands a `checklist:` input to the port as a
`{key, path, content, digest}` envelope. ADR-0170's Rust smoke handler deserializes `checklist` as a list. The real
`RustToolchain` answered *"smoke refused (input-malformed): the `checklist` input: invalid type: map, expected a
sequence"*. The `demands::` test double hid this because it parsed the envelope itself.

**Ruled** (blocker panel, option (a) refined):
1. **Placement.** A `Toolchain` decorator inside `rk-cli::verbs::run::real_toolchain`, so every composition path gets
   it. A test or lint asserts that no adapter constructor in rk-cli is reachable undecorated.
2. **Trigger.** Only the `checklist` input of a `run_verb` call, and only when it is an object whose `key` starts with
   `checklist:` and which carries a string `content`.
3. **Transform.** Parse `content` with `rk_kernel::toml_subset`; no new TOML dependency is added. The `[[item]]` rows
   become a JSON array. The journalled input keeps its envelope and digest.
4. **Fail closed.** Each of these refuses as input-malformed, naming the key:
   - an unparseable envelope;
   - a missing or empty `item` array;
   - an object that fails the trigger;
   - any shape other than an object or an array.

   An input that is already an array passes through unchanged.
5. **Tests.** The `demands::` double becomes strict and sits behind the same decorator. The red-first tests are:
   - (i) a real `RustToolchain` smoke call through the decorator over the shipped `smoke.toml` gets past input
     parsing;
   - (ii) the undecorated call still refuses;
   - (iii) malformed content refuses by name.

   A mutant removes the decorator and must be killed.
6. **Record.** ADR-0194 (proposed) takes a dated note. It also names an unreconciled point: ADR-0170 decision 8's
   `(id, text)` checklist rows against the smoke checklist's item form.

**Reversal.** Remove the decorator. If a second toolchain verb comes to read a checklist, take option (c), where the
resolver hands over the array.

---

## D21-125 · `s8g` B2: the `init` and `status` smoke rows stay out; ADR-0170 decision 4's internal contradiction is named, not moved

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-09-29, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-28616f** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to A). No accepted Decision moves. The panel's full ruling is in the
run's judge-panel output (s8g package S8G-P7's items B1–B4), summarised here.

scratch is an empty directory, and ADR-0170 decision 4's handler runs no git.

2. **No inverted rows.** Option (b), rows pinning exit 3, is rejected: they would certify a refusal as health.
3. **The contradiction is named, not moved.** ADR-0170 decision 4's escape clause (*"a row that needs the directory to
   be a repository makes it one in its own argv"*) cannot be met under the same decision's rule (*"against the
   binaries that build produced, never an installed `redkiln`"*). ADR-0194 (proposed) and the review packet record it.
   The two rows are owed to whichever record makes the smoke scratch a repository.

**Reversal.** Write that record, then measure and add the two rows.

and neither adapter lints Gherkin or docs. So both refuse on the story lane.

## D21-128 · Checkpoint 6's Command-site lines: the test git helpers route through `git_exec`, and the production line is narrowed to its fourteen pinned non-git spawn sites

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-09-30, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-4f3d23.** It is authority-bound because the `s8d` close flagged this question for the operator, so the weigh-in item is
**left open** for the operator. The work proceeds on it, and it is on the review packet's confirm list.

**The blocker.** `s8g`'s refuting round 1 raised it. Checkpoint 6's two Command-site bans have been red since 7c and 7d,
and Checkpoint 8g (12) replays Checkpoint 6 whole. The `s8d` close recorded: *"Whether Checkpoint 6 is re-run, and
against which narrowing of its Command-site line, is **flagged for the operator and not decided**"*. Measured:
- **The git line** reads 21 at `bb22b51` and 19 once `s8g`'s two new sites are routed. The 19 are 16 test helpers
  (redkiln-migrate and rk-cli `tests/it`) and 3 lint-control fixtures.
- **The production line** (`crates/*/src`) reads 14. All 14 are non-git process spawns, in 8 files:
  - rk-adapter-claude `runner.rs` (2);
  - the two toolchain `run.rs` files (2 each);
  - rk-cli `doctor/probe.rs` (1);
  - rk-containment `bounded.rs` (2), `ffi.rs` (2), `posix.rs` (1) and `windows.rs` (2).

**Ruled.** The blocker panel chose a hybrid split by warrant. Checkpoint 6 stays in Checkpoint 8g (12); dropping it is
rejected.

**(A) The git line.**
- The 16 test-helper sites route through `rk_git::git_exec::run` or `run_with_env`. Shared bodies collapse onto one
  support helper, and each `#[expect]` reason is corrected.
- Checkpoint 6's git line gains, by dated D21-47 edit, only the anchored exclusion `ci.yml` already carries
  (`tests/fixtures/lint-controls/`), with the three controls pinned.
- The line then reads 0. **ADR-0077 is kept whole**, and no record moves.

**(B) The production line.** It is narrowed to its warrant by a dated in-place edit under D21-47, on the precedent of
`s6`'s 2026-09-13 note (a).
- The regex is kept, and an anchored admission list names the 8 files, each with a PINNED per-file count compared by
  strict equality (14 in total).
- Each entry names its raising stage (7c for the adapters and rk-containment, 7d for the doctor probe) and gives
  ADR-0127 as the reason: rk-containment owns spawning, and its accepted API takes a `std::process::Command`.
- The clippy `disallowed-types` deny remains the per-site instrument.

**(C) Controls, in a private worktree only.** Each must turn its line red:
- (i) a new git site in a new tests file;
- (ii) a new git site in a routed file;
- (iii) a widened exclusion with a planted fixture;
- (iv) a new spawn in an unlisted src file;
- (v) an extra spawn in an admitted file;
- (vi) a removed admitted site.

**(D) Census on this ledger.**
- The git line: 21, then 19, then 0, with 3 pinned controls.
- The production line: 14, all admitted and pinned.

PROGRESS row 6's 7c/7d mark and its `s8g` mark are discharged only by an rc-0 pass of Checkpoint 8g (12).

**Fallback, if the routing cannot land in `s8g`.** Option (1): admit the test sites with pinned per-file counts,
mirrored into `ci.yml`, and write a `proposed` record against ADR-0077's *"anywhere else in the workspace"*.

**Reversal.** Revert the routing and the admission list, and take the fallback, or drop Checkpoint 6 from (12).

*(dated 2026-09-30, s8g build, package D21128-8g.)* **Built.** A1 and A2 are done: the 16 test-helper git spawns
route through `rk_git::git_exec::run`, over one support helper per test binary. For rk-cli that helper is
`support::git_run`, with `git_ok` and `git_code` built on it. For redkiln-migrate it is `git_ok`.

**The A2 measurement.** `git_exec::run` maps a non-zero exit to `Err(GitError::NonZeroExit { code, stderr, .. })`
and keeps no stdout on failure.
- **The five helpers that read a failing exit** read `code` off the error, or treat the error as the refusal they
  already expected: `init::git_ignores`, `lane_e2e::git_read`, `lane_e2e::git_rc`, `run_worktree::git_rc` and
  `slice_verdict::seals_on_run_ref`. Each keeps its meaning, and four mutants, one per exit-reading arm, were all
  killed:
  - M1 (`git_code` reads a non-zero exit as 0): 2 failed;
  - M2 (`git_ignores` reads exit 1 as ignored): 1 failed;
  - M3 (`git_read` reads a refusal as empty output): 6 failed;
  - M4 (the seal reader panics on a refusal): 2 failed.
- **The seven helpers that ignored the status** now panic on failure: `support::head_message`,
  `support::commit_count`, `main::refs`, `main::porcelain`, `main::common_dir`, `answer::author_of` and
  `root::root_of`. The rk-cli suite stayed green under that change (310/0/0), so no caller reads a failed git's
  stdout.
- **No site is unroutable, so A5 did not fire and no ADR was written.**

**The census.**
- The git line: 21 at `bb22b51`, 19 at `66c9c6c`, and 0 after A1 and A3, with 3 pinned controls.
- The production line: 14, all admitted and pinned, with 0 unadmitted.

**Controls (i) to (vi)** were each red (rc 1) and then green (rc 0) in a private worktree. `s6` note (c) records
them.

**Checkpoint 8g gains (11b).** It is a direct probe of both lines. It passes on the routed tree, and it FAILED
(rc 1, 16 sites) over the unrouted `66c9c6c` tree.

**Checkpoint 6, whole, extracted from `s6`'s bytes:** three formal runs through the no-dispatch wrapper at 4/4, extracted from this file's bytes (no line of it runs a redkiln binary or mutates git, so none was skipped), each exited 1 at a whole-suite cargo test line on ONE test, the 5 s-bound `run::ac403_gate_open_writes_trailer_and_event_and_exits_zero_without_waiting` (6.2 s, 6.4 s and 9.4 s, each 309 passed / 1 failed), with the operator's IDE running; re-run alone it passes (1 passed, 1.57 s). A DIAGNOSTIC copy skipping only that test in the two whole-suite runs exited 0 and reached its end marker, S-GIX line printed, so every other line, both Command-site lines included, passes. NOT a formal pass: Checkpoint 8g (12)'s replay owns that.

**Measured and not disposed of: `ci.yml`'s mirror is still red.** The ruling's A3 premise was *"ci.yml's git grep
also reads 0 with no ci.yml edit"*.
- On this tree, the step's `bad=` pipeline reads **4**. All 4 are COMMENT lines naming the token:
  - `rk-adapter-toolchain-node/tests/it/ac334.rs:22`;
  - `rk-cli/tests/it/tag_release.rs:83`;
  - `rk-cli/tests/it/verify_pass.rs:2300`;
  - `rk-store/tests/it/contention.rs:18`.
- The step's own comment says *"The trailing `grep -vE` drops COMMENT lines"*, but its pipeline carries no such
  filter.
- `ci.yml` is unedited here. The disposition goes to the panel.

*(dated 2026-09-30, s8g build, package R2FIX-8g; refuting round 2's major.)* **(B)'s census function had put
`git ls-files` LEFT of a pipe**, Halt 8's PIPE-STATUS shape. `python runbook/tools/check-checkpoints.py` printed
`runbook/phase-1/s6-store-and-git.md:255  [PIPE-STATUS]  git ls-files 'crates/*/src/*.rs' ...` and `flagged 1`
(it read `flagged 0` at `178868f`), which breaks INDEX check 4 (`# exit 0`) and Checkpoint 8j (9). The census
is rewritten in Halt 8's own capture form, by a dated in-place edit in `s6` under D21-47:
`cp6_prod_files=$(git ls-files ...); rc=$?`, with its rc and non-emptiness asserted at top level, and the
function pipes the capture through `printf`. The regex, both exclusions, the admission table and the three
planted controls are unchanged, and a fourth planted control (an empty listing) must refuse. The checker's
allowlist is NOT widened (still 20 entries). Measured after the edit: `scanned 7006 executable lines in fenced
bash blocks; flagged 0; allowlist 20 entries`, rc 0. The Checkpoint 6 Command-site lines, extracted from `s6`'s
bytes and run read-only, exit 0. Three controls exit 1 on their own FAIL lines: an empty listing, a failed
listing (`exited 128`), and a planted spawn in an unlisted file (the census inequality). The ruling itself does
not move.

## D21-129 · `s8g` closes: the verify pass is the one `TestReport` route and journals `verify_completed`, the schema exports `AcceptanceCriterion`, the four demands have producers, and `tag-release` enters by trial

**Taken 2026-10-02 by the session executing `s8g`, at the stage's close, under the operator's standing AFK rules
(D21-105).** It records and cites. It re-takes no ruling and writes no go-ahead line. The stage's six records,
ADR-0191 … ADR-0196, flip under D21-105 item 1, which carries D21-64 ruling 10 into the AFK session, each by its own
dated ledger line (D21-131 … D21-136), not by this entry. ADR-0190, `s8f`'s telemetry-key record held under D21-107,
flips on D21-130, on the operator's confirmation beneath D21-107. What this entry records is what `s8g`'s §8g.1 …
§8g.11 record; every figure below is quoted from the stage file's recorded readings, the tree, the committed records
or the refuting rounds' outputs, except the formal reading in the last paragraph, which is quoted from the run's
output.

Records: R1 = ADR-0191 (the verify record), R2 = ADR-0192 (the schema record), R3 = ADR-0193 (the write-set record),
R4 = ADR-0194 (the producers record), R5 = ADR-0195 (the tag record), and ADR-0196 (the Findings-key record, D21-119),
each written `proposed` at the id free at writing (§8g.8's dated notes, packages S8G-P1-RECORDS and R-D2119-8g).

**Recorded — the six records, and how they flip.** Each id was recorded at write time, R1 … R5 in Checkpoint 8g (2)'s
`R<n>_ID` lines and ADR-0196 in (6b)'s `FK_ID` line (D21-47: *"Records are located by the id recorded at write
time"*); each record is `status: proposed` at `4777941`, in ADR-0085's form with its `### Option — ` headings and
**What this forbids** block. The README's next free number reads **`0197`**, and the set is 196 records.
- **ADR-0191** (R1) supersedes in part **ADR-0162**'s forbids bullet's patch half (*"forbids applying a mutation patch
  from `rk-cli`"*), for the verify gate's declared AC mutations alone; **ADR-0132**'s keyed-set enumeration (D21-121:
  *"The toolchain-keyed set is **six**"* and *"moves 5 → 6 keyed kinds"*, both becoming seven with `AuthoredTests`);
  and **ADR-0189** decision 8's member list, for one additive `key` member on the keyed writer's row (D21-122). The
  back-link on `0162` was written with the record (Checkpoint 8g (2) asserts both directions before the flip); the
  back-links on `0132` and `0189` were not, and D21-131's flip commit writes them, with their README status cells and
  two Supersessions rows. Its title names `0162` only; D21-131's flip commit adds a dated title correction naming
  `0132` and `0189`, and moves nothing else in the record's text.
- **ADR-0193** (R3) supersedes in part **ADR-0105** clause 5's closed list, for `tag` and `apply`, beside `0180`
  decision 15 and `0188` decision 10, the other two partial supersessions of the clause. Its back-link on `0105` was
  written with it.
- **ADR-0196** supersedes in part **ADR-0186**'s `Findings`-first key sentences and its inert-seal gap statement
  (D21-119). Its back-link on `0186` was written with it, with a dated reading note in ADR-0186.
- **ADR-0192** (R2), **ADR-0194** (R4) and **ADR-0195** (R5) supersede nothing. ADR-0194 carries five dated notes
  (D21-124 … D21-127 and an evidence note); ADR-0192 decision 2 carries D21-123's note.
- **Checkpoint 8g (2) and (6b) admit `status: (proposed|accepted)`**, and no line of Checkpoint 8g, nor of the blocks
  its (12) replays, asserts `status: accepted` for this stage's records (§8g.1's entry reading: *"FLIP_PENDING is not
  needed"*). So the formal reading below ran with all six `proposed`, and no `FLIP_PENDING` line was written for them.

## D21-130 · ADR-0190 flips to `accepted` — `dispatch_completed` carries `activity_id_at_version`, `cost` breaks an item's owned rows down by activity, `status` renders draws through `cost`'s one renderer, and `doctor` reads the store's journal

**Confirmed 2026-10-02 on the operator's confirmation beneath D21-107 (Weigh-In wi-838696 decided A) and on the
operator's standing authority (D21-105 item 1, which carries D21-64 ruling 10 into the AFK session), by the session
executing `s8g`.** ADR-0190 (`s8f`'s record, held at `s8f`'s close) flips `proposed → accepted`, `date: 2026-09-28`
unchanged. It was written `proposed` at `s8f`'s build (package S8F-P1-RECORDS) and revised in place while it bound
nothing (dated in-record notes from packages S8F-P7-TELEMETRY-COST-DOCTOR-JOURNAL, R1FIX-8f-A-CODE and
R2FIX-8f-B-RECORDS, under D21-107). It was held at `s8f`'s close by D21-107: *"ADR-0190 does not flip at `s8f`'s close
while this entry is unconfirmed (it moves two accepted records' Decisions); it stays `proposed` under D21-49's
flip-stop and goes on the review packet's confirm list."* The confirmation reads: *"This lifts the entry's hold:
ADR-0190 flips to `accepted` at `s8g`'s close under D21-105 item 1, on `s8f`'s two conditions (the formal Checkpoint
8f passed whole at `ae38553` with ADR-0190 held, and refuting round 8 was dry), with its four `superseded_by`
back-links (0131, 0138, 0163, 0174) and Checkpoint 8f (2)'s `FLIP_PENDING` emptied in the same commit."* *"The
operator also confirmed Checkpoint 8f (2)'s `FLIP_PENDING` form at the same sitting: Weigh-In wi-f613b1 decided A."*
The rulings read:
D21-105 item 1: *"each stage's own records flip at that stage's close under ruling 9's two conditions (the formal
checkpoint passes whole, and a refuting round finds no blocker or major), each on its own dated ledger line citing
this entry."* D21-64 ruling 10: *"Standing authority for the rest of this session (`s8d` onward): each stage's own
records flip at that stage's close under the same two conditions, each with its own dated ledger line citing this
entry. This lifts D21-49's flip-stops for those records."* The two conditions are ruling 9's, read for `s8f`'s
Checkpoint 8f.

Both conditions hold, as `s8f`'s. The formal Checkpoint 8f passed whole at `ae38553` with ADR-0190 held by (2)'s
`FLIP_PENDING` line (D21-116, its last paragraph), and `s8f`'s refuting round 8, the AFK rule's cap, was dry: no
blocker, no major (`2acb35a`). Checkpoint 8g (12) replayed Checkpoint 8f whole in the formal Checkpoint 8g reading at
`4777941` (D21-129, its last paragraph: rc 0, 0 `FAIL`, `CP8G-END-OF-BLOCK reached`), with ADR-0190 still held there.

The text that flips is the one at `4777941`; the flip commit changes its `status` line only. No Decision clause and no
forbids bullet moves. The supersession in part, for `dispatch_completed.activity_id_at_version` alone and until
ADR-0131's tag is wired, of ADR-0131's first forbids bullet, four passages of ADR-0163, three passages of ADR-0138 and
two of ADR-0174 (its decision 9) is now in force; each of the four stays `accepted`. ADR-0099 decision 1 is read, not
superseded: the declared reading is confirmed with D21-107. No entry follows D21-128 before this close, and none of
D21-105 … D21-128 records an objection (measured over the ledger's text from D21-105 on, the conditions' own wording
set aside). **The flip commit writes the four `superseded_by` back-links**, `0190` appended to ADR-0131's, ADR-0138's,

R-D2122-D2123-8g, R1FIX-8g and R2FIX-8g, under D21-120 … D21-123, each TAKEN PROVISIONALLY). The rulings read:

## D21-137 · `s8h`: §8h.6's settings go to the child as a file, `--settings <path>`, written per dispatch outside the worktree; ADR-0197 (proposed) decision 9 amended in place

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-10-02, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-10f1da** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to A). ~~No accepted Decision moves: the one sentence that changes is ADR-0197
(Record A) decision 9, which is `proposed`, so it is amended in place by a dated edit and no superseding record is
owed.~~ The ruling is on the review packet's confirm list.

*(dated 2026-10-02, s8h build, package R2FIX-8h; s8h refuting round 2, records finding 1.)* **Corrected: one
accepted Decision moves, in part.** Item 4 puts `settings_file { path, sha256 }` on the dispatch's
`DispatchRecord`, on its `dispatch_completed` row (`crates/rk-types/src/activity.rs`:
`pub settings_file: Option<SettingsFile>,`). ADR-0126 (accepted) decision 1's payload table lists that record
member by member, and Record A's decision 12 states the rule: *"A member added to a `## Decision` table is a
supersession (ADR-0092 rule 1)"*. So this ruling moves ADR-0126 decision 1 in part, beside `observed_model`. It
moves through Record A's existing supersession (ADR-0197's frontmatter already reads `supersedes: ["0126",
"0074"]`, and the back-link on ADR-0126 is written in 0197's flip commit), so **no new record is owed**. ADR-0197's
title, preamble and decision 12 now name both added members, and the README's 0197 row says the same, each by
dated edit. The operator reads this correction with the ruling on the confirm list.

**The blocker.** Package S8H-P3 raised it.
- §8h.6 and ADR-0197 decision 9 put the child's settings, including the deny-rule complement for the worktree's
  ancestor chain, in *"one `--settings <json>` element"* on the argv.
- RT-1 caps the argv at 32,767 UTF-16 units. P3 measured the inline JSON on 2026-10-02:
  - about 11,989 units for `.redkiln/worktrees/bug-1` (37% of the cap, before the schema and the card);
  - about 1,068,619 units for a worktree under `%TEMP%`, where every adapter and rk-cli test repository lives;
  - about 243,048 units under `target/tmp`.
- Wiring it would refuse every test bind with `ArgvAtOrOverCap`, and production binds as the operator's directories
  grow. P3 built and tested `command::ChildSettings` but held the wiring: `Binder::bind` and `dispatch` pass
  `settings: None` under dated interim comments (interim 2, `5e5d410`).

**Ruled** (blocker panel, option 1 with the judges' hardenings, unanimous 9/9/9; the record is
`target/afk-wf/s8h-ruling-d21-137.json`):
1. **Deliver a file.** The argv elements are `--setting-sources`, `""`, `--settings`, `<path>`, in the same position.
   `<path>` names a file whose bytes are exactly `ChildSettings::json()`. The rule set, `ConfinementChain`, the block
   setting, `autoMemoryEnabled: false` and the stated residual are unchanged. The CLI documents the form: *"--settings
   <file-or-json>  Path to a settings JSON file or a JSON string"* (2.1.287 `--help`).
2. **The directory is a caller input** (`settings_dir`), never discovered by the adapter. rk-cli `run` passes
   `<common_dir>/redkiln/runs/<run_id>/` and creates it before the first dispatch; tests pass a tempdir outside the
   worktree.
   ***AMENDED 2026-10-02 (Weigh-In wi-f940f2, provisional under D21-105; blocker panel, unanimous; the record is
   `target/afk-wf/s8h-ruling-d21-138.json`):*** *the location is `<common_dir>/redkiln/child-settings/<run_id>/`, a
   sibling of the run record keyed by the same `run_id`. `runs/<run_id>/` could not hold item 3's retention clause:
   `rk_kernel::replay::write_run` begins `let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);` and rk-cli calls it after the
   stage's dispatches. A run's evidence is now the pair `runs/<run_id>/` (the record, replaced whole by
   `write_run`) and `child-settings/<run_id>/` (launch evidence, never deleted), joined by `run_id` and by each
   `dispatch_completed` row's `settings_file { path, sha256 }`. `read_run`, replay and `explain` read none of it,
   so ADR-0130 (accepted) is untouched. A guard test, `run::a_child_settings_file_survives_write_run_for_its_run_id`,
   pins the move, with a mutant that points the directory back at `runs/`. Residual, fail-closed and left to the
   operator: `run_id` is content-derived and the directory is never emptied, so an identical re-run can meet the
   same file name (a recycled PID, the sequence restarting, identical bytes). `create_new` then refuses before
   spawn and nothing is overwritten. The fixes (a per-invocation subdirectory, a nonce, or accepting identical
   bytes) each move something §8h.6 pins. The directory is also unbounded. Whether `remove_dir_all` removes a
   read-only file on Windows is unmeasured; it matters only if the location moves back to `runs/`. The adapter
   never runs git to find the directory and has no default.*
   *(dated 2026-10-02, s8h build, package R1FIX-8h; s8h refuting round 1.)* The `remove_dir_all` residual above is
   measured: M-D21-137-loc's run (`s8h` §8h.13) pointed the directory back at `runs/<run_id>/`, and the read-only file
   `write_settings_file` wrote there was gone after `write_run`. It does delete it; the read-only flag is no
   protection, which is why the location moved.
3. **Fail closed, by type.** Each of these refuses before spawning, and none falls back to `settings: None`:
   - a missing directory;
   - a directory under the worktree, compared case-folded on Windows with `\\?\` stripped;
   - a path that is not absolute or carries a verbatim prefix;
   - JSON over 2,097,152 bytes, the CLI's settings-file limit (read from the binary by string search, not executed);
   - any write failure.
4. **Write per dispatch.** The file is written with `create_new` under a unique per-dispatch name and marked
   read-only. Its sha256 and path go in the dispatch's launch evidence, and it is kept with the run. `Binder::bind`
   writes nothing: it builds the settings, checks the 2 MiB limit, and measures the argv against RT-1 with a stand-in
   path of the dispatch form.
5. **Red-first tests**, each guard with a killing mutant:
   - a 5,000-sibling synthetic chain assembles under the cap;
   - the file's bytes equal `json()` and the recorded sha256 matches;
   - the under-worktree, missing-directory and over-2 MiB refusals;
   - two dispatches make two files;
   - `%TEMP%` and `target/tmp` binds succeed.
6. **Unmeasured until `s8j`** (D21-105 item 2), each owed to a named probe arm:
   - probe 2: the file argument is honoured;
   - probe 1: the outside-read refusal under file delivery, plus a Read of the settings file itself, which must be
     denied;
   - a missing or unparsable path must fail closed;
   - a size arm near 2 MiB.
   If the CLI ignores the file, D21-42 21d applies: the work HALTS.
7. **Not adopted, kept as possible probe arms:** option 3's negated-class patterns and 2.1.287's `--restricted`.
   Neither is a fence of record.

**Unmoved:** D21-42 21b and 21d, RT-1, D21-45 and ~~every accepted ADR~~ *(dated 2026-10-02, s8h build, package
R2FIX-8h)* every accepted ADR but ADR-0126 decision 1, which moves in part through ADR-0197 as corrected above.

---

## D21-138 · `s8h` closes: the cards bind at least privilege, models are pinned by full id, schemas are read from `EXPORTED`, the child's settings go to it as a file, and one launcher is the only entry to a real run; both records stay `proposed`

**Taken 2026-10-02 by the session executing `s8h`, at the stage's close, under the operator's standing AFK rules
(D21-105).** It records and cites. It re-takes no ruling, writes no go-ahead line, and **flips no record**: ADR-0197
(Record A) and ADR-0198 (Record B) both stay `proposed`, each for the reason its own text gives (below). What this
entry records is what `s8h`'s §8h.0 … §8h.15 record; every figure below is quoted from the stage file's dated notes,
the tree, the committed records or the refuting rounds' outputs, except the formal reading in the last paragraph,
which is quoted from the run's output.

Records: Record A = ADR-0197 (cards, models and the run environment) and Record B = ADR-0198 (RT-57's pack scalars,
the per-item envelope and gate expiry), each written `proposed` at the id free at writing, in that order, by package
P1-records (§8h.1's dated note), and each recorded at write time in Checkpoint 8h's INPUTS lines, `REC_A=0197` and
`REC_B=0198` (D21-47: *"Records are located by the id recorded at write time"*).

**Recorded — the two records, and why neither flips at this close.**
- **Record A (ADR-0197)** supersedes in part ADR-0126 decision 1 (its payload table gains `observed_model` and, by
  D21-137, `settings_file { path, sha256 }`) and ADR-0074 decision 1 (its finding shape gains `severity`). Its
  back-links on `0126` and `0074` are not written, and Checkpoint 8h (2) fails if either is (*"the link moves in the
  flip commit (ADR-0092 cl.2.1)"*). It does not flip here. Its decision 13: *"This record flips only after both `s8h`
  §8h.11 probes pass at `s8j`, on the operator's dated ledger line (D21-41 (g): "The record is accepted only after two
  live probes pass"), and after the operator has signed decision 7's table enum by enum."* Checkpoint 8h (2) asserts
  it: *"Record A must be proposed until both s8j probes pass (D21-41 (g))"*. The probes are spend outside S-BIN, which
  D21-105 item 2 defers to the review packet.
- **Record B (ADR-0198)** supersedes nothing. It does not flip here either. Its decision 8: *"The operator signs
  decision 2 value by value and decision 6 row by row on a dated ledger line. This record flips to `accepted` at
  `s8h`'s close, before `s8i`, on that line"*. §8h.1 says the same (*"it flips to `accepted` on the operator's dated
  ledger line **at this stage's close, before `s8i`**"*), and §8h.15 names the signatures *"a stage-close gate
  (AC-276: "routed to a human gate; not machine-decidable")"*. The stage's Closing puts them before the tracker
  commit: *"three gates are passed, and none can be decided by a machine"*. No machine can give that line, and the
  operator is AFK, so the signature goes on the review packet's deferred-gate list. Record B flips on the operator's
  signing line, when it is written, and not on a line this session writes. D21-105 item 1 carries the standing flip
  authority to `s8h`'s records under ruling 9's two conditions; both hold (below), but Record B's flip clause names
  the signing line as the line it flips on, and this entry does not read item 1 as replacing it. The reading is on the
  review packet for the operator to confirm or reverse.
- **No `FLIP_PENDING` form is needed** and none is added: Checkpoint 8h (2) asserts `status: proposed` for Record A
  and `status: (proposed|accepted)` for Record B, and no line of the block asserts `status: accepted` for either
  (§8h.15's dated note, package P8). The formal reading below ran with both records `proposed`. The README, the
  preamble census and every predecessor's `superseded_by` line are unchanged at this close.
- **What holding Record B holds.** §8h.14 hands on to `s8i`: *"Record B must be accepted before S-BIN spends (D21-42
  19c)"*, and `s8j`'s evidence check refuses while it is not (*"RT-57's record (ADR-…, s8h's Record B) is not accepted
  (D21-42 19c)"*). So `s8i`'s S-BIN spend, the only spend D21-105 item 2 authorises, waits on the operator's signature
  of Record B, or on the operator's reversal of this entry's reading.

**Recorded — the provisional rulings this stage carries.** Each was TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105)
pending the operator's confirmation, recorded in the weigh-in ledger, and is on the review packet's confirm list.
- **D21-137** (`wi-10f1da`, defaulted A): §8h.6's settings go to the child as a file, `--settings <path>`, written per
  dispatch with `create_new`, read-only, outside the worktree, because the inline JSON exceeds RT-1's 32,767-unit argv
  cap (about 11,989 units for `.redkiln/worktrees/bug-1`, about 1,068,619 under `%TEMP%`). Every failure is a typed
  refusal and none falls back to `settings: None`. Corrected by dated line at R2FIX-8h: one accepted Decision moves in
  part, ADR-0126 decision 1, through Record A's existing supersession (`settings_file` beside `observed_model`), so no
  new record is owed.
- **D21-137 item 2, amended** (`wi-f940f2`, defaulted A; ledger line `ed6dc02`): the directory is
  `<common_dir>/redkiln/child-settings/<run_id>/`, not `runs/<run_id>/`, because `rk_kernel::replay::write_run` begins
  *"`let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);`"*. The guard
  `run::a_child_settings_file_survives_write_run_for_its_run_id` pins it, and its mutant M-D21-137-loc measured that
  `remove_dir_all` does delete the read-only file on Windows. Its residuals are recorded and fail closed: an identical
  re-run can meet the same file name (`create_new` refuses before spawn), and the directory is unbounded.

## D21-139 · The operator signs ADR-0198 (Record B) value by value and row by row; ADR-0198 flips to accepted

**Signed 2026-10-02 by the operator, in the operator's own words in the session that records it.** This is the
operator's dated signing line that ADR-0198 decision 8 and AC-276 require, and ADR-0198 flips `proposed → accepted` on
it. It is not a provisional ruling under the AFK rules of D21-105: no Weigh-In defaulted it, and the session that
writes it down takes no ruling of its own. ADR-0198 decision 8: *"The operator signs decision 2 value by value and
decision 6 row by row on a dated ledger line. This record flips to `accepted` at `s8h`'s close, before `s8i`, on that
line"*. AC-276, as ADR-0198 quotes it: *"routed to a human gate; not machine-decidable"*. D21-42 19c: *"The scalar
record is written and signed before any spend"*, and `s8i` spends, so the line comes before `s8i`.

**The signature, verbatim.** The values and the rows were laid out with their warrants, and the operator answered two
questions:
- on decision 2: *"Sign all six as drafted"*;
- on decision 6: *"Sign all 56 as fail"*.

**Signed: decision 2, value by value.** Each is signed as drafted, with the warrant decision 2 gives it:
- `appetite.dispatches = 40`;
- `appetite.tokens = 24000000`;
- `gate_deadline_secs = 864000`;
- `[breaker] consecutive_platform_failures = 3`;
- `[breaker] expiry_secs = 600`;
- `[margins] five_hour = 10` and `seven_day = 10`.

**Signed: decision 6, row by row. All 56 rows are `fail`, with no exceptions.** Rows 1 to 56 of decision 6's table are
each signed `fail`: the 45 catalog question classes, the seven `unknown_facet:<axis>` classes, `escalation`, and the
three kernel-minted classes `landing.refused-path`, `render.structured-drift` and `verify.ac-discriminates`. No
`escalate` exception is signed and no row is `auto-decide`. So Checkpoint 8h (8)'s `EXCEPTIONS` stays empty, and both
installed packs' `[[gate_expiry]]` rows (54 in `bug-standard`, 56 in `story`) stand as shipped.

**What moves in the flip commit.**
- ADR-0198 flips `proposed → accepted`, and `date: 2026-10-02` is unchanged. It supersedes nothing, so no
  `superseded_by` back-link moves. Only its `status:` line moves. Its body does not, as `s8h`'s Closing requires
  (*"the frontmatter, the README index cell and the census move, and the body does not"*), so decision 6's `signed`
  cells stay as drafted, and this line is their signature, row by row.
- `TOKENS_APPETITE` stays `24000000`. The signed appetite equals the draft, so decision 8's dated edit of Checkpoint
  8h's `TOKENS_APPETITE` is not owed, and Checkpoint 8h's block does not move.
- `references/adr/README.md` moves ADR-0198's status cell, the lifecycle census (192 → 193 `accepted`, 2 → 1
  `proposed`), the not-at-accepted sentence and the open-questions sentence, each by dated correction. The
  not-at-accepted sentence was stale through `s8h`'s build and now reads five, with `0197` at `proposed`.
  `runbook/00-preamble.md` carries no status census, and the flip moves neither its record count nor its next free
  number.
- `runbook/PROGRESS.md` gains this entry's §2 row and a dated note in row 8h. The `s8h` stage file gains a dated note
  after its close paragraph. `s8i`'s stage file carries no precondition line naming Record B, so it takes no receiver
  note.
- 7 prose lines under `runbook/` and `references/`, outside every fence and the record files, call ADR-0198
  `proposed`, and each takes a dated note naming this line. The comments under `crates/` and in `.redkiln/packs/` that
  read *"ADR-0198 … proposed"* are left (39 lines), because tests read those bytes: `cards.rs` counts `ADR-0198` in
  each pack. The flip script lists them.

**What this lifts.** D21-138 held Record B: *"Record B flips on the operator's signing line, when it is written, and
not on a line this session writes."* This is that line, so D21-138's hold on Record B is lifted. Record B flips on its
own decision 8, not under D21-105 item 1, so D21-138's reading of item 1 is not exercised. §8h.14's hand-on to `s8i`
is met: *"Record B must be accepted before S-BIN spends (D21-42 19c)"*. `s8j`'s evidence check refuses while *"RT-57's
record (ADR-…, s8h's Record B) is not accepted"*, and it no longer refuses on that ground once its evidence file names
`0198`. Record A (ADR-0197) is untouched and stays `proposed` under its decision 13.

**Checkpoints.** Checkpoint 8h (2) asserts `status: (proposed|accepted)` for Record B, so it holds on both sides of
the flip. No line of any fenced block under `runbook/` asserts ADR-0198 `proposed` (measured at the flip over 5 fenced
`status: proposed` lines, none of which reads Record B's file). Checkpoint 8h is re-run whole over the flipped tree as
`s8i`'s entry gate. The flip is checked by running Checkpoint 8h's INPUTS and (2) from the stage file's own bytes over
the flipped tree, with two controls, and the four runbook validators.

## D21-140 · The operator raises S-BIN's spend cap to US$90 (amending D21-105 item 2) and routes `s8i`'s corpus build through a private copy and a patch

**Taken by the operator on 2026-10-02** in the session running the AFK chain, with the trade-off in view, answering two
questions. This is the operator's own ruling, not a D21-105 provisional one.

**1. The cap.** Asked *"S-BIN's batches likely exceed your US$30 cap (B1 ≤ ~$10.50 + B2 ≤ ~$25 at their bounds; B3,
retries and B4 after; the stage file's own cap is $90). What should the cap be?"*, the operator answered **"Raise to $90
(file's cap)"**.
- D21-105 item 2's *"capped at **US$30 total**"* now reads **US$90 total**, for S-BIN's dispatches only. Every other
  live or paid step stays deferred, exactly as item 2 lists them.
- The figure is the stage file's own registered cap (`s8i` §8i.5: 18 Opus, 3 haiku, $90 API-equivalent).
- AFK rule 2 still applies. Before each batch, the orchestrator sums the spend so far from the dispatch cost records,
  and a batch launches only if (90 − spent) exceeds that batch's configured bound. The spend table lives in the stage
  file and in the review packet.
- Every S-BIN launch is the operator's, made from a plain terminal and behind its own go-ahead line (`s8i` §8i.1; D17-5's
  foreground half). No subagent dispatches.

**2. The corpus.** Asked how to build the S-BIN driver and audit, given that the stage file reserves corpus writes to the
operator, the operator answered **"Private copy + patch"**.
- The driver (`runner/src/sbin.rs`) and `audit-s.py bin` are built and tested in a private `git archive` copy of the
  corpus's `main` under `target/`.
- The operator receives a patch with its apply and commit commands.
- Nothing writes to `D:/repos/redkiln-corpus` or its `.git`. `s8i` §8i.3's *"Every write to D:/repos/redkiln-corpus is
  the operator's"* holds unchanged.

**Effect on `s8i`.** The amendment's item 10 registers the driver's USD cap at 90.00, citing this entry, and the spend
table's cap column reads 90.00.

## D21-141 · `s8i`: Checkpoint 8i (8)'s kernel type scan blanks string literals before it matches, as Checkpoint 8a's `k_filter` does

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-10-02, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-45f99f** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to A). No record moves.

**The defect.** Package S8I-P1-OPEN measured it read-only at `09018dd`. Checkpoint 8i (8)'s `KT_RE` scan drops comment
lines, but it does not blank string literals. So it fires on two rk-kernel code lines that hold the artifact-kind
string, not the type:
- `crates/rk-kernel/src/lattice.rs`: `&& ["Changeset", "ImplReport", "ChangeSummary"]`;
- `crates/rk-kernel/src/workflow/memo.rs`: `Self::SummariseChanges => "ChangeSummary",`.

Checkpoint 8a's `k_filter` blanks string literals before it matches, which is why 8a passes on the same lines. As
written, (8) cannot pass on today's tree.

**Ruled.** (8)'s scan blanks string literals before it matches, by dated edit in the `s8i` build. A synthetic control
admits a string-literal line, so the blanking is shown to work, and a second control shows a real type use is still
caught. Not taken: a counted exemption of the two lines, and renaming the literals in rk-kernel. The rename would be a
kernel change no stage owes, and it would make 7b, 7b-ii and 8a provisional.

**Id note.** The stage-open note reserved D21-141 for the S-BIN entry that holds the go-ahead lines. That entry takes
the next free id when it is written, and the reservation note is corrected by dated line.

*(dated 2026-10-03, operator sitting, s8i step 1)* **The operator REJECTS this provisional ruling and takes (B), a counted exemption.** Grounded first by a read-only panel (two lensed judges and a synthesiser): the blanking admitted three real type uses on synthetic lines (a raw string with an inner quote, the closing line of a multi-line string, `r"\"`), which none of the block's seven controls covers; (C) is ruled out because `"ChangeSummary"` is wire vocabulary (`spikes/p9/registry.toml:10`). Ruled: (8)'s `kt_scan` loses its string and char blanking; exactly the two lines above are exempt, matched on path plus the whole trimmed line (never a line number), each entry matching exactly once and exactly 2 admitted; new F controls for a type use appended to an exempt line, exempt content at another path, a duplicated exempt line, each of the three probes, and a stale entry. It is built by dated edit before the formal Checkpoint 8i, with one refuting round scoped to (8); it moves no DRV, AUD or dry-run byte. Correction: the block has seven controls, not the two this entry names, and it also blanks char literals, which 8a's `k_filter` does not.

*(dated 2026-10-06, s8i build, D21149-8i-C)* **(B) built, proposed.** Checkpoint 8i (8) is edited by dated edit in `runbook/phase-1/s8i-s-bin.md`. `kt_scan` blanks no string or char literal. `KT_EXEMPT` names exactly the two lines above by path plus whole trimmed line, and the scan prints any entry matched other than once and any admitted total other than 2. The controls: 14 must fire (the four kept F controls; a type use appended to an exempt line; exempt content at another path; a duplicated exempt line; the three probes; each entry stale; one entry twice with the other stale; a third entry) and 3 must be admitted (the two exempt lines alone, the doc comment, the `change_summary` method call). Run read-only from the stage file's bytes over `crates/rk-kernel/src` at `36dd8ac`: pass, all 17 controls pass. Against the superseded blanking scan, 9 of the 17 fail. Of twelve in-memory scan mutants, ten are killed. Two survivors are recorded: the trailing-CR trim, which is equivalent because Git Bash's grep strips CR, and emptying the real-scan line, which is unobservable over a clean tree. No DRV, AUD or dry-run byte moved. The refuting round scoped to (8) is still owed.

white-on-white"**.

## D21-143 · `s8i`: S-BIN's go-ahead lines, the entry Checkpoint 8i reads them from (OPEN)

**OPEN. Drafted 2026-10-03 in the AFK build, package S8I-P8. Nothing in this entry is a go-ahead until the operator
signs a line in it.** It takes no ruling. It is the entry `s8i` §8i.5 opens: *"in the ledger entry this stage opens
(next free D21 id at writing)"*. Checkpoint 8i (0) records its id as `SBIN_LEDGER=D21-143`, written by dated edit on
the day this entry is written (D21-47: ~~*"a record is located by the id recorded when it was written"*~~ *"Records are located by the id recorded at write time"*; *(dated 2026-10-03, R1FIX-8i: the struck words were a paraphrase in quotation marks)*).

**The id.** The stage-open note reserved D21-141 for this entry. The METHOD-S `s8i pre-dispatch amendment` (introduced
at `285b659`) says *"The go-ahead lines for B1–B4 are written by the operator into ledger entry D21-141, reserved for
S-BIN"*. D21-141 and D21-142 were taken first, and D21-141's id note reads *"That entry takes the next free id when it
is written, and the reservation note is corrected by dated line."* So this entry is D21-143. The amendment is
append-only and is not edited. The correction is recorded here, and by dated note in the stage file.

**What binds every line.**
- **The spend.** D21-105 item 2 as amended by D21-140 item 1: *"D21-105 item 2's "capped at **US$30 total**" now reads
  **US$90 total**, for S-BIN's dispatches only."* Before each batch the orchestrator sums the spend so far from the cost
  records: every record's `total_cost_usd`, `results/s-bin/probes.jsonl`, and the procedure log's `cost_usd`. A missing
  cost is refused, never read as zero. A batch launches only if (90.00 − spent) > its bound in `s8i` §8i.5's spend
  table: B1 10.50 (probes ≤ 0.50 in all, each capture session ≤ 2.00), B2 25.00, B3 25.00, each retry 5.00, and B4
  20.00. At the bounds, B4 does not fit.
- **The launch.** D21-140 item 1: *"Every S-BIN launch is the operator's, made from a plain terminal and behind its own
  go-ahead line"* and *"No subagent dispatches."*
- **The fixture proofs, on the B1 line only.** D21-43 N22: *"S-BIN is gated on Checkpoint 8h plus its zero-spend dry
  run, confirmed on the B1 line."* Checkpoint 8h passed WHOLE at `d24d15a` (D21-138; `PROGRESS.md` row 8h). It was
  re-run green after the Record B flip, at `b2ab1dc`. The B1 line names `d24d15a`, which row 8h records and which
  Checkpoint 8i (5) resolves.
- **A refusal stops the batch.** D21-40 decision 2: *"If a real run is refused unexpectedly, the next step is to stop
  and report, not to spend again."*

**How to sign.** Each line below is in `s8i` §8i.5's fixed form. To sign a line, the operator replaces its `<…>`
placeholders **in place**, with the UTC time at which it is written, immediately before the batch. A line is never
duplicated. Checkpoint 8i (5) reads every `S-BIN go-ahead B<n>` line in this entry. It needs exactly one line each
for B0 to B3, and one for B4 if and only if a re-run exists, and it fails an unsigned line as `BADFORM`. B2's and B3's
lines are added in the same form before those batches, and B4's only after B4's amendment. `caps used so far` is the
summed spend, as `$<x.xx>`.

**The dry run's sha256 on the B1 line is owed.** The dry run was prepared and not run in this build (`s8i` §8i.3's

## D21-144 · `s8i`: the S-BIN driver's out-of-tree audit is a hardened, measured denylist at runtime, with an allowlist shadow audit that holds flagged records unscored

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-10-03, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-35d9f8** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to A). It is ruled by the blocker panel, 2-1, with the dissent recorded below.
The record is `target/afk-wf/s8i-ruling-d21-144.json`. No accepted ADR moves. METHOD-S's amendment item 4 changes only
by an appended extension, R5FIX-8i.

**The blocker.** Five refuting rounds over the driver alternated between two failure modes:
- **False voids**, each a paid Opus dispatch thrown away. R3FIX audited every string and voided 59/754 honest corpus
  records (7.8%).
- **Missed reads**, each a seeded answer scored as a detection. R4FIX audited reads only, against a denylist of
  answer-bearing roots, and round 5 found reads that escape it:
  - a path carried by an assignment and read through `$VAR`;
  - a recursive read of an ancestor (`grep -r /d/repos`, or Glob/Grep on `D:/`);
  - copies of seed-stating files outside the registered roots;
  - the `/c/` and `~` spellings of the session's own tool-results.

**Ruled.**
1. **The runtime void, which voids and applies §8i.6's halt, is R4's reads-only lexer plus four additions:**
   - assignment tracking and `$X` expansion, with assignment values audited;
   - the ancestor rule: a recursive read rooted at or above a registered root reads that root;
   - one spelling fold applied before every containment test;
   - named roots taken from a **measured read-only sweep** of the reachable drives for the seed tokens, the seed and fix
     blob oids and the fixture's seed statements. The expected set is D:/r6c, D:/repos/redkiln-sev and %TEMP%/claude,
     plus the roots already registered.
2. **A shadow audit that never voids, retries or counts toward caps.** It applies an allowlist over the kept raw
   transcript: the copy slug's tool-results, OS and toolchain roots, and the UE engine install, which is measured from
   B1 and provisional until then. Temp counts only where the session wrote the path first. An unresolved `$VAR` is
   flagged.
   - A flagged record is **held unscored** until the operator clears or voids it, at the item-4 gate before B1 and
     before each later batch is scored.
   - An operator void is `void: out-of-tree`, gets no AFK retry, and does not count toward the second-void halt.
3. **Acceptance:**
   - every round-5 must-catch case is a red-first named test;
   - the 754-record corpus replay stays at 0 false voids;
   - every 838-transcript void is checked as a genuine read;
   - a killing mutant exists per rule.
   - If the sweep finds copies it cannot bound, or the denylist still leaks, escalate to B as the runtime void by a
     separate proposed extension. Never slide into it silently.

**Dissent.** Judge 3 would make B the runtime void, since only an allowlist fails closed. The shadow hold answers that
for scoring, and judge 3's position is the named escalation. Judge 1 objects in part to a human-held step. The flag
itself is mechanical, and the operator's clear-or-void step is the item-4 confirmation already owed.

**Operator housekeeping before B1** (deferred gate, deletions or moves): move D:/r6c and the redkiln-sev copies off
reachable paths, and clear %TEMP%/claude task outputs. This lowers both the false-void surface and the leak surface.

*(dated 2026-10-03, s8i build, R5FIX-8i)* **Built, and one point returned to the orchestrator.** The sweep ran
read-only over 5,066 start points on C: and D:. 397 files hold a hit, bounded by eleven roots: `D:/r6c`,
`D:/repos/redkiln-sev`, `~/.claude-personal`, `~/.claude-backup` (an account copy holding BrittonGames' session
transcripts), `~/AppData/Local/JetBrains`, `~/AppData/Local/Packages/Claude_pzs8sxrjxfjjc`, and five under the temp
directory, `claude` among them. The copies are bounded, so no escalation to B follows. 17 hits state no seed and are
not registered, a judgement named in METHOD-S's R5FIX-8i extension. **The point returned:** registering
`<temp>/claude` voided 64 of the 838 replayed sessions, each reading its own background-task output, which the CLI
keeps at `<temp>/claude/<cwd slug>/<session>/tasks/`. The ruling did not foresee that the child writes there itself.
The build exempts the dispatch copy's own slug's `tasks/` PROVISIONALLY, in R4FIX-8i item 3's shape, so that every
acceptance measurement could run. The shadow audit still flags those reads. Whether the exemption stands is a ruling
owed.

*(dated 2026-10-03, s8i build, R6FIX-8i (refuting round 6's minors))* **The point returned is ruled by D21-145**
(TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under D21-105): the own slug's `tasks/` is exempt in every spelling. D21-145 does not decide
whether a pre-spawn check proves that directory absent, or whether the shadow allowlist carries it; both stay with the
operator at item 4's gate (METHOD-S's R6FIX-8i extension, item 1). **The counts above are corrected** (that
extension, item 5) [measured: `s8i_R6FIX-8i_sweepcount.py` over `R5FIX-8i-sweep-out/`, output
`R6FIX-8i-sweepcount.out`]. The sweep's outputs hold 397 unique hit paths: 378 under the eleven roots, and 19 outside
them. The 19 are 17 paths to mypy's `report.py` and one Google DriveFS content-cache copy of that file, listed under its
own path and under the profile's legacy `AppData/Local/Application Data` junction. *"17 hits state no seed"* counted
16 of the `report.py` hits and the work-OneDrive document, which came from a separate pass. Every unregistered hit still
states no seed, and no registered root moves.

*(dated 2026-10-03, operator sitting, s8i step 1)* **The operator CONFIRMS A; no escalation to B.** Grounded by a read-only panel over the final replay (`R7FIX-8i-replay-r7final.out`): under A, 71 runtime voids of 838 sessions (70 genuine, 1 disclosed false); under B, 241 further honest sessions would void, and every leak rounds 6 to 8 found escaped both audits alike. Owed item (g)'s replay half is discharged **by class**: the historical replay flags (10,794 R5SHADOW-FLAG lines in the final replay, not the R5 build's 11,064) are acceptance evidence only, cleared by the class table, and get no line in `results/s-bin/oot-shadow-rulings.jsonl`. S-BIN records are ruled line by line, defaulting to void.

*(dated 2026-10-03, operator sitting, s8i step 2)* **Housekeeping (h) done by the operator, by deletion** (the operator's choice; a move elsewhere on C: or D: would stay reachable and outside the registered roots). Deleted: `D:/r6c` (a 2026-09-25 scratch clone, HEAD `b8e7aa9`, with staged scratch edits), `~/.claude-backup` (a 2026-08-02 account backup), every `%TEMP%/claude` entry except this orchestrating session's own folder (`D--repos-redkiln-rs/d93cf9d3-…`), and the temp files the sweep named (`tmp.uDZZzvzEF4`, `h.md`, `l143`, `ms0`). Verified after: the targets are absent, and no `D--sb-*` slug exists under `%TEMP%/claude` (D21-145's absence check, first reading: OK). By the operator's split, `D:/repos/redkiln-sev`'s seed-bearing tooling (`afk-tools/sp`, `afk-tools/close-templates`) is removed after packet step 4 and before the B1 line is signed, because steps 2 and 4 read it by pinned path.

*(dated 2026-10-05, operator sitting)* **(h)'s `redkiln-sev` half, pruned before the B1 line** (the operator's choice, "Prune now, rest at close"). Deleted from `D:/repos/redkiln-sev/afk-tools/sp`: 95 directories under `s8i/` (the private corpus copies `S8I-*-corpus` and `S8I-P4-pristine`, the replays `S8I-*-replay`, the `*-patchwork`, `*-ctl` and `*-fmtwork` build copies, `S8I-P6-verify`, every `*-mutant-logs` and every `refute*` directory), `s8g/`'s 6 leak-token files, and `s8i-plan.json`. `sp/s8i` went from 62 GB to 265 MB. Kept, under the registered voiding root until `s8i`'s close: the scripts, the patches, `S8I-P7-results` (the greenness and pin reports and the staged fixture, owed to the corpus), `S8I-P8-*`, `S8I-D2114x-out`, the sweep output and the `*-before` snapshots (149 files still hold a leak token), and `afk-tools/close-templates` and `workflows`. D21-145's absence check reads OK (no `D--sb-*` under `%TEMP%/claude`).

## D21-145 · `s8i`: the reviewer's own background-task output under `<temp>/claude/<own slug>/<session>/tasks/` is exempt from the out-of-tree void

**TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under the AFK rule (D21-105) of 2026-10-03, pending the operator's confirmation — Weigh-In
wi-94b181** (two-way door, judgment; defaulted to A). No record moves. The ruling is registered in the R5FIX-8i METHOD-S
extension.

**The blocker.** Package R5FIX-8i raised it. D21-144 (d) registers `%TEMP%/claude` as a runtime root, because other
sessions' task outputs there quote seed names. But the CLI also writes a reviewer's OWN background-command output to
`<temp>/claude/<cwd slug>/<session>/tasks/<id>`. The 838-transcript replay voided 64 honest sessions for reading it.

**Ruled.** That own-slug `tasks/` path is exempt, in every spelling, exactly as R4FIX item 3 exempts the session's own
tool-results. Other slugs under `<temp>/claude` stay answer-bearing. A background command that itself reads outside the
copy is audited when it is issued, so the exemption hides nothing the audit has not already seen. The 838 replay then
reads 71 voids: 70 genuine, and 1 disclosed false void, a mistyped path to the session's own task output.

*(dated 2026-10-03, s8i build, R6FIX-8i (refuting round 6's minors))* Three corrections.
- **Where it is registered.** The exemption is built in the R5FIX-8i METHOD-S extension's item 4, which still calls it
  provisional and a returned blocker. METHOD-S is append-only, so this ruling is registered by the R6FIX-8i extension's
  item 1, and item 4 is not edited. *"No record moves"* stands.
- **What it decides.** The exemption itself, and nothing else. Item 4's other two questions stay with the operator at
  item 4's gate: whether a pre-spawn check proves `<temp>/claude/<copy slug>/` absent, and whether the shadow allowlist
  carries the path. As built, there is no such check, and the shadow audit flags those reads.
- **The cite *"D21-144 (d)"*.** The letters (a) … (d) are the ruling record's (`target/afk-wf/s8i-ruling-d21-144.json`),
  not this ledger's. Here (d) is D21-144's Ruled item 1, its fourth bullet: *"named roots taken from a **measured
  read-only sweep** of the reachable drives"*, whose *"expected set is D:/r6c, D:/repos/redkiln-sev and %TEMP%/claude"*.

*(dated 2026-10-03, operator sitting, s8i step 1)* **The operator CONFIRMS the exemption and answers its two questions, with no driver change.** (1) The absence proof is the operator's: after housekeeping (h), and before each batch B1 to B4, from a plain Git Bash, `ls /c/Users/ryanm/AppData/Local/Temp/claude | grep -i '^D--sb-'` must print nothing (0 of 257 entries matched on 2026-10-03). (2) The shadow allowlist does not carry the path; those reads stay flagged, and a flag whose only evidence is a read under `<temp>/claude/<copy slug>/<the record's own session id>/tasks/` is cleared. Correction: a background command is audited after the session, over the transcript's tool uses, not *"when it is issued"*.

## D21-146 · The operator rules that the review trees' editor launcher is neutralised in all five trees before B1

**Taken by the operator on 2026-10-03** at a Weigh-In sitting (wi-938b68, decided A), with the evidence and options laid
out: *"Neutralise in all 5"*. This is the operator's ruling, not a D21-105 provisional one. It resolves owed item (i),
recorded by R8-RECORD-8i after refuting round 8.

**The residual.** Every base tree ships `_workspace/tooling/RunEditor_OurGame.bat`, whose line 13 reads `set
PROJECT=D:\repos\BrittonGames\OurGame\OurGame.uproject`. The tree documents it as the way to launch the editor, and
running it escapes both the runtime out-of-tree void and the shadow audit.

**Ruled.**
1. The launcher joins §8i.2 item 3's registered strip set, by an appended METHOD-S extension, identically in all five
   trees. It is either removed or rewritten to a tree-relative PROJECT, whichever keeps the stripped set identical
   across trees; the fix package chooses and states which.
2. The five base trees are rebuilt at zero spend, with the leak-token, blob-oid and strip checks re-run.
3. The driver gains a precheck that refuses a copy holding an absolute answer-bearing path in an executable file
   (.bat, .cmd, .ps1, .sh, or Python outside docstrings), with a named test.
4. The tooling scripts whose absolute BrittonGames paths sit only in docstrings are measured and stated. They are not
   stripped unless the precheck finds a path in executable code.
