## D21-147 · The operator rules that one generated backlog stub is written into all five review trees, and that the greenness helper's `UnrealTraceServer` survivor is not Halt 6

**Taken by the operator on 2026-10-03** at a sitting in the session that walks `s8i`'s operator steps, with the evidence
grounded by a read-only panel (two lensed judges and a synthesiser). This is the operator's ruling, not a D21-105
provisional one.

**The defect.** The operator's greenness run of clean item 2 (`fa1ea72f4`) read 920 tests, 2 failed, exit 1. Both are
`OurGame.Architecture.NoInertCapability` (*"Expected '8.3.5a exists in the backlog' to be true"*, *"Expected 'the
backlog JSON parsed and yielded stories' to be true"*). The spec reads `_workspace/backlog/game_foundation_backlog.json`
at test time, and `_workspace/backlog/` is in amendment item 3's registered strip set, whole. The spec exists in bases 2
and 3 only; items 1, 4 and 5 lack it. Item 1 read 892 tests, 0 failed, exit 0. The registration requires green only on
the seeded trees (`s8i` §8i.4 step 2), but the report is each item's `tests.report_ref`, so two strip-caused failures
would reach the reviewers of two of the three clean items and no one else, and METHOD-S counts two of three clean items
blocked as `undecided-fp`. Restoring the real file is ruled out by measurement: at every item commit it carries the
registered leak tokens (`LoadoutFill` 2 to 5 times, `white-on-white`, `VERIFIED COLD`).

**Ruled.**
1. After the existing whole-directory strip, one generated file is written at
   `_workspace/backlog/game_foundation_backlog.json`, byte-identical in all five trees: `{"stories":[...]}` holding
   exactly the ids the NoInertCapability specs read, `8.3.5a`, `8.3.5b` and `8.5.8`, each with `"status": "todo"`, and
   no other field or story. It is registered by an appended METHOD-S extension, which discloses it as fixture content:
   at item 3's commit `8.3.5a` and `8.5.8` read `done`, and item 3's spec reads only `8.3.5b`. No further story is
   admitted (`8.5.5b` tracks the seed).
2. The driver writes and checks it: the strip writes the stub, the identical-strip-set test admits exactly that one
   file, and a named check refuses a copy whose stub differs from the registered bytes. A new driver patch supersedes
   `S8I-D21146-8i-sbin-driver.patch`.
3. The five bases are rebuilt at zero spend, with the leak-token, blob-oid, LFS and strip checks re-run; the dry run's
   review repository is rebuilt from the new base item 1, and its commit and fixture hash move; all five greenness runs
   are redone on the new bases (item 1's 892/0 and item 2's red are superseded). One refuting round follows before B1.
4. **The greenness helper's Halt 6.** `s8i_S8I-P7_green.py` matches any process name containing `unreal`, which is
   broader than the registered rule (an `UnrealEditor` process: `UNREAL_PROCESS_PREFIX = "unrealeditor"` in the driver;
   packet step 12's `^"unrealeditor`). It stopped items 1 and 2 on `UnrealTraceServer.exe`, UE's shared trace daemon,
   with no `UnrealEditor*` surviving. That is not Halt 6. Before and after each greenness run the operator confirms no
   `UnrealEditor*` process and ends `UnrealTraceServer.exe`; the helper is not changed.
5. **Disclosed.** Grounding this ruling, one panel judge wrote the five item commits' full backlog JSONs to
   `%TEMP%/bl_*.json`, new answer-bearing copies outside the registered roots after housekeeping (h). The orchestrator
   deleted them the same evening, before any dispatch.

## D21-148 · The operator rules that tree 5's headless verify script is BrittonGames' later Monolith-disabling blob, identical in all five trees, and that item 5's red is an instrument defect, not a red seed

**Taken by the operator on 2026-10-04** at a sitting in the session that walks `s8i`'s operator steps, grounded by a
read-only panel (two lensed judges and a synthesiser). This is the operator's ruling, not a D21-105 provisional one.

**The reading.** The operator's greenness run of seeded item 5 (R = `6a101bdcd`, base `e0620bfa`) read 175 tests, 2
failed, exit 1. Both are `OurGame.Combat.AttackRootMotion.BlendAttackRootMotionVelocity`, and their failure events hold
no spec assertion: only `LogBlueprint` asset-load errors from the ALS-era notifies (`BP_AnimNotify_FoleyEvent`,
`BP_NotifyState_MontageBlendOut`), whose `/Game/AdvancedLocomotionV4/` dependencies `fb0f856f7` removed before R. The
Monolith plugin's first-launch deep indexer loaded them while those tests ran (1,383 `LogMonolithIndex` lines). The
seed asset appears 0 times in the log.

**The cause is the instrument.** Bases 1 to 4 carry `_workspace/tooling/verify_headless.ps1` blob
`ebe7e58f3df5756ad016d9075512a27cd2417fae`, from BrittonGames `71e8f6c91` (2026-08-18, *"stop Monolith running inside
the headless test editor, where it can fail any test"*), which passes `-DisablePlugins=Monolith`; their logs show the
flag twice and 0 indexer lines. Base 5 carries R's blob `a2047f2d836d2d48e1f37c2145a2f1328091736f`, which predates it.
The five runs did not use one instrument. The suite did not catch the seed, so §8i.4 step 2's *"A red seed is
replaced"* does not apply: this is not a red seed. Replacing seed 5 would not help: every qualifying candidate
(`9ca831a42`, `6a101bdcd`, `f6269ae7e`) predates `71e8f6c91`.

**Ruled.**
1. Tree 5's `_workspace/tooling/verify_headless.ps1` is set to blob `ebe7e58f…`, so that all five trees carry one
   identical script. It is registered by an appended METHOD-S extension, which discloses that base 5's tooling file
   differs from R's. The registered greenness command (§8i.4 step 1) is unchanged; the fix is in the tree, so a
   reviewer who runs the suite in its copy runs it with Monolith disabled too.
2. Base 5 alone is rebuilt at zero spend, with its leak-token, seed blob-oid, LFS, strip, D21-147 stub and exec-path
   checks re-run. Bases 1 to 4 and their green reports stand. The seed and R do not move.
3. Item 5's greenness is re-run **once**, and that run binds; the red run is kept as superseded, with this diagnosis.
   It must read 175 tests. If it is still red with Monolith disabled, the cause is in R's tree and it returns to the
   operator.
4. If the newer script fails on R's tree for a reason inside the script itself, the fallback is a one-line backport
   of `-DisablePlugins=Monolith` into R's script, disclosed as not byte-identical across trees.

## D21-149 · B1's capture step meets an asset type it cannot render: the operator rules that the tool's own rejection gives an empty capture list, identically on every tree

**Taken by the operator on 2026-10-05**, at a §8i.6 stop in B1, grounded by a read-only panel (two lensed judges and a
synthesiser). This is the operator's ruling, not a D21-105 provisional one.

**The stop.** B1's capture session for item 2 (`D:/sb/cap/item-2`, Opus 5.5, **$0.90**) ran the registered
`/ue-verify /Game/Tests/FTEST_Inventory_Stash — write the capture_scene_preview of the k-th asset above to
_s-bin/captures/capture-<k>.png`. The map resolved, PIE ran on it and stopped, and no capture was written:
`capture_scene_preview` rejects a World. `MonolithEditorActions.cpp:2765`, byte-identical in all five bases, returns
*"Unsupported asset_type: %s (supported: niagara, material, static_mesh, skeletal_mesh, widget)"*. Item 3's two assets
(`BP_GamePlayerState`, a Blueprint class; `DA_Item_Sword`, a data asset) are unsupported too, read from the same source
but not yet measured in a session. §8i.0 item 11 left this *"Unmeasured"*; §8i.6 makes it a stop for a ledger line, and
METHOD-S's amendment item 2 step 5 says *"nothing falls back to empty captures"*. Item 1 captured as registered
(`WBP_StashRow`, $0.98, `shows_subject=yes`). Spent so far: $1.90 (probe $0.021026; items 1 and 2).

**Ruled (B).**
1. Every changed asset still gets the registered `capture_scene_preview` call, unchanged ($ARGUMENTS and the capture step
   as registered). When the tool rejects an asset's class, that asset carries no capture. An item where every asset is
   rejected carries `captures: []`; its procedure-log line reads `sha256=none shows_subject=n/a` with the session's model
   and `cost_usd`. A partly previewable item carries a capture for each accepted asset, matched to its asset by explicit
   index. The tool's rejection decides, never a per-tree choice, so N19 holds.
2. Registered by an appended METHOD-S extension that amends item 2 step 5 and discloses the confound: capture presence
   follows asset type, and both seeds are widgets (P(seeded | capture) = 2/3 over the five items), which F43 must state
   beside the false-positive outcome. Each reviewer sees only its own item, and clean item 1 carries a capture.
3. The driver accepts an empty capture list and renders it neutrally; captures carry an explicit asset index; the dry
   run no longer injects a synthetic capture; `every_item_carries_a_capture_reference_or_rendering_refuses` is inverted,
   red first. That is a new runner commit, a new zero-spend dry run, and a re-signed B1 line naming it, all before B2.
   Checkpoint 8i's `caplog_ok`, its synthetic controls and the per-item capture pins are amended by dated edit.
4. Order: item 2's procedure-log line is written now (`n/a`, $0.90, so its cost stays in the cap accounting); item 3's
   session runs under the unchanged step so its rejection is measured; items 4 and 5 are captured under the unchanged
   step; then the driver change, the dry run and the re-signed line. Before B2, item 2's and item 3's constructed specs
   are checked for an acceptance criterion that names a capture.

*(dated 2026-10-06, s8i build, D21149-8i-A)* **Item 3's driver change, item 2's extension and item 3's checkpoint amendments are built (proposed, unsigned).** The driver patch is `S8I-D21149-8i-sbin-driver.patch` (`a904b4c0…e637`), and the inverted test is renamed `captures_name_their_asset_index_and_a_rejected_asset_has_none`. The dry run takes item 1's real capture, and METHOD-S's D21149-8i-A extension is appended (items 1, 2, 4 and 6). Checkpoint 8i's `caplog_ok`, its controls and the per-item capture pins are amended by dated edit (`s8i` §8i.3, §8i.5, §8i.9). Still owed: the runner commit, the new dry run and the re-signed B1 line (the operator's, §8i.5's packet), and item 4's spec check (owed item (e)).

*(dated 2026-10-06, s8i build, D21149-8i-B)* **Item 4's check, run on the constructed specs (owed item (e)).** No acceptance criterion of item 2 (`chore-ftest-inventory-stash`, 5 ACs) or item 3 (`8.3.9-install-routing-configuration`, 6 ACs) names a capture: `captur`, `screenshot`, `ue-verify` and `png` occur nowhere in either spec or report (`results/s-bin/spec-item-<n>.md`, staged under `S8I-D21149-stage/`, METHOD-S's D21149-8i-B extension). Nothing is edited for it.

## D21-150 · Item 5's capture session stalled on the instrument: the operator rules one retry on a fresh capture copy

**Taken by the operator on 2026-10-05**, in B1. This is the operator's ruling, not a D21-105 provisional one.

**The reading.** Item 5's capture session (`D:/sb/cap/item-5`, editor PID 3020, Opus 5.5, **$0.76**) loaded
`/Game/UI/HUD/WBP_GameVitals` cleanly, then lost Monolith during `start_pie`: port 9316 stopped listening while the
editor stayed alive and responding, after Monolith's indexer had logged *"Critical memory situation detected (<2GB
available)"*. No capture was written and nothing was substituted. The launcher read `RC=0`, CLI 2.1.289 after. The
editor recovered (9316 listening again, 5.1 GB free) after the session exited. This is an instrument stall, not a
framing judgement and not a type rejection (D21-149), so `shows_subject=no`'s §8i.6 stop does not describe it, and the
registration provides no second session for an item.

**Ruled.**
1. The stalled session gets its own procedure-log line, `sha256=none shows_subject=stalled`, with its model and
   `cost_usd`, so its cost stays in the cap accounting.
2. The editor is closed (PIE stopped first if it is running), Halt 6 is checked, the copy and its temp slug are
   removed, and memory is freed. Item 5 then gets **one** retry on a **fresh** capture copy from base 5 (a copy is never
   reused), under the unchanged registered step. The retry's line binds. A second stall is a stop for a ruling.
3. Checkpoint 8i's `caplog_ok` (already owed a dated amendment by D21-149) also admits exactly one `stalled` line per
   item ahead of that item's binding line, and sums every line's `cost_usd`.

*(dated 2026-10-06, operator sitting)* **The stall's cause, measured on the retry.** It was not memory. `start_pie` opens UE's modal *"Blueprint Asset Compilation Error … BP_AnimNotify_FoleyEvent"* (the ALS-era notify that R's tree still carries, D21-148), and the modal blocks the game thread, so Monolith stops answering. On the retry the operator dismissed it with *Play in Editor*; PIE ran on `TestOpenWorld` and `stop_pie` returned `{"stopped":true}`. The retry (Opus 5.5, $1.00) captured `WBP_GameVitals` before PIE, `capture_scene_preview` type widget, `capture-1.png` `0de695357e86f170ed59f40432634988ecc8ba9ddd2071033d3bfc5f261bf026`, `shows_subject=yes` (the operator's judgement on framing). B1 is complete at $5.561026 spent.

## D21-151 · Owed item (f): the operator rules that the driver checks free space before every dispatch copy

**Taken by the operator on 2026-10-06**, before B2. This is the operator's ruling, not a D21-105 provisional one.

**The gap.** METHOD-S amendment item 4: *"Before each copy, the free space on the root's volume must be at least
`SBIN_NEED_BYTES`"*. The driver does not check it (`s8i` §8i.3, *"Not built here"*), and §8i.5's pre-check reads free
space once per batch. Owed item (f) left the choice with the operator: build it, or register that the batch pre-check
carries it (METHOD-S's R2FIX-8i extension, item 5).

**Ruled.** The driver checks it before every dispatch copy, retries and B4 re-runs included, against the registered

## D21-152 · The operator accepts the five constructed spec/report pairs as registered, diagnosis included, with an F43 disclosure and a recorded-only evidence-source tag

**Taken by the operator on 2026-10-06**, before B2 and before any rendering is hashed, grounded by a read-only panel
(two lensed judges and a synthesiser, which split). This is the operator's ruling, not a D21-105 provisional one.

**The question.** Owed item (e)'s five pairs (METHOD-S's D21149-8i-B extension) render each report as *"the commit
message rendered"*, so the two seeded items' reports carry the fix commit's own diagnosis: item 4's quotes *"alpha was
0.097 where 0.97 was intended"* and names the fill widget; item 5's carries the three R tokens and names cold
verification as the missing check. The review tree carries none of them (amendment item 3).

**Ruled (K).**
1. The five pairs are accepted exactly as built (report sha256 `9ae0c522…`, `a916fa90…`, `00696b45…`, `f0a9f60f…`,
   `8848e41c…`, with their spec hashes as the D21149-8i-B extension tables them). No report byte moves. Grounds: the
   diagnosis does not reach the tree, so detection still means reading the asset's binary state; the ChangeSummary and
   the spec already name the seeded asset and property by design; E3's fix-lane reports describe the pre-fix state the
   same way, so S-BIN stays comparable with E3; and any redaction chosen now would be chosen after the B1 captures, for
   its effect on the seeded items (§3.6's author-chosen threat).
2. **F43 discloses it:** the seeded items' reports carry the commit's own diagnosis (the widget, the pre-fix value or
   state, and for item 5 the cold-verification method); a §3.4 branch-1 result reads as *"sufficient given the
   report"*, not *"ChangeSummary alone sufficient"*; and branch 2's signal is less likely for the same reason.
3. **A recorded-only tag**, applied when F43 is written and never in scoring: for each blocking finding, whether its
   evidence cites something read from the asset (an editor, Python or binary read, or a capture) or only strings that
   appear in the spec or report. §3.4's decision rule is unchanged.
4. Registered by an appended METHOD-S extension. The panel's fallback, a cut of all five reports at `## Notes`, is
   recorded as considered and not taken.

## D21-153 · S-BIN's pass-1 set is discarded at the §8i.6 stop; the fixture is fixed and re-registered, and B2 and B3 re-run under a new go-ahead

**Taken by the operator on 2026-10-07**, at the §8i.6 stop recorded under D21-143 (*"The same item voids twice in one
pass — a fixture defect, not noise"*), grounded by a read-only panel (two investigators, two lensed judges and a
synthesiser). This is the operator's ruling, not a D21-105 provisional one.

**The finding.** Both of item 1's voids are genuine reads of BrittonGames. The motive: item 1's acceptance criteria
are runtime-only, and the trees carry no binaries and no base checkout. The destination: every review tree names the
real project's absolute path, in `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` (the Rider `rootFolder` line) and in further docs under
`_workspace/` (`Rider_CoDev_Setup.md`, `UE58_MCP_Setup.md`, references and runbooks); the registered leak tokens cover
the seeds only. Attempt 1 ran `git show cc0cf2d01` inside BrittonGames. Pass 1 can never be scored (`next_attempt`
returns `VoidedTwice` for item 1), and every real fix changes an item or the rendering, which §8i.6 makes a discard.
The audit also keeps an out-of-tree `cd` across later commands (`sbin.rs` `audit_reads`), where the CLI resets the
shell's cwd; it inflated item 1's void lists (25 and 64 entries, about 5 and 3 real) and changed no record's status.

**Ruled (1): discard, fix, re-register, re-run.**
1. **The pass-1 set is discarded.** Corpus `07bd6a2`'s six records ($16.074148) stay committed and are moved aside as
   superseded; they are not scored. Their spend counts against the cap.
2. **(a) The path leak is removed.** By a registered mechanical rule applied identically to all five trees, every
   absolute spelling of the BrittonGames path is removed from the trees' text files, and those spellings join the
   registered leak tokens, so the pre-spawn leak check refuses a tree that still holds one. The rule is the build
   package's to state and measure; a rule that would change a file the build or suite reads returns to the operator.
3. **(b) The motive is answered in the rendering.** It states plainly that the review has no runtime, no PIE, no
   built binaries and no base checkout, and that any read outside the review tree voids the record. The operator
   accepts knowingly that this bears on what `reproduced` can mean for a runtime-only criterion, and F43 says so.
4. **(c) No system isolation.** BrittonGames stays where it is; with (a) and (b), any read that still reaches it voids
   as today. The snapshot procedure is unchanged.
5. **(d) The audit's cwd model follows the CLI**, red first: a `cd` inside the copy (or into temp the session wrote)
   is kept, and a `cd` outside it is audited as the read it is and then reset, so later relative words resolve in the
   copy.
6. **(e) A new arm or records file**, so that pass 1's `VoidedTwice` does not carry into the re-run.
7. **(f) Rebuilt at zero spend:** the five bases and review trees, the fixture, a new runner commit, the B0 block,
   a new dry run, and refuting rounds. Greenness is re-run if any changed file is one the build or the suite reads.
8. **Caps.** The $90 cap and the 18-Opus dispatch cap stand, counting the discarded set. At the bounds a re-run of
   B2 and B3 leaves too little for B4; that is accepted, and B4 runs only if (90 − spent) still exceeds its bound.
9. A new go-ahead line is signed for each re-run batch under D21-143, after the dry run.

Checkpoint 8i (4) needs at most three probes, a haiku `observed_model` on each, and no null cost. (3) No `--max-budget-usd`: the 300 s kill is the probe's only bound. (4) If the probe contradicts item 5's temp parenthetical, *"(or into temp the session wrote)"*, the item's lead, *"The audit's cwd model follows the CLI"*, governs for temp, and the audit is built to the measured rule with a dated note. Any other divergence from the driver's current model returns to the operator: OLDPWD or the `pushd` stack persisting across calls, `cd -` moving, a directory outside the copy kept, or a drive-relative `cd` read otherwise than as `x:/`. *(dated 2026-10-08, operator sitting)* **The operator's rulings after the cwd probe** (taken by the operator on 2026-10-08; recorded here, not proposed). The probe ran under the signed line above (2026-10-09T04:41:07Z; $0.0676585; its readout is in `D:/repos/redkiln-sev/afk-tools/sp/s8i/cwdprobe/out/cwd1/`). On CLI 2.1.289: a `cd` inside the copy persists to the next call; a `cd` into temp, whether the session wrote it or not, is reset silently to the cwd the call started in; a `cd` outside the copy, a drive-relative one included, is reset to the copy root with the CLI's notice; the `pushd` stack does not persist; and every call's OLDPWD is the value inherited at launch, so `cd -` reaches the launching shell's directory. (5) Under ruling (4), the temp clause follows the measurement. (6) `cd -` and OLDPWD: neutralise and audit. The driver spawns claude with OLDPWD set to the dispatch copy, and the audit resolves `cd -`, `~-`, `$OLDPWD` and `${OLDPWD}` to the OLDPWD the record carries, voiding one outside the copy or unknown. (7) Step 0m's fixed form gains `SPAWN` (D21153-8i-G's amendment), accepted. (8) Under H-12, the moved expectation in the cwd test (the written-temp case, from persist with 0 hits to reset with 1 hit) is approved. The line below is step 0m's.

D21-153 cwd rule: CLI 2.1.289 measured temp RESET, OLDPWD and pushd stack SPAWN; patch 61cfac85b16a5ad678c507814690fab08c599b7b88ace219f80e53648a8739b9

*(dated 2026-10-09, operator sitting)* **The operator's rulings after refuting round 12** (taken by the operator on 2026-10-09; recorded here, not proposed). (9) The bare-`cd` residual is closed. A `cd` with no non-option operand (`cd`, `cd -P`, `cd -L`, `cd --`) is `cd ~`. It is audited as the read it is; OLDPWD becomes the cwd it leaves, and the cwd becomes the home directory. Later relative reads therefore resolve under home, and void under the sweep and answer-bearing roots. METHOD-S's D21153-8i-H extension supersedes R4FIX-8i item 6's bare-`cd` residual line. (10) The real-read minors are fixed void-safe. The audit keeps OLDPWD in step with bash: `popd` sets OLDPWD before it moves; any assignment to OLDPWD (bare, prefix, or through `export`/`declare`/`local`/`typeset`/`readonly`) sets it to the expanded value, and the spawn's value no longer overrides it; declaration-builtin values are tilde-expanded like bare assignments; and `~-` after `=` or `:` is recognised. These are voided, not modelled: a bare `pushd`; a `pushd` or `popd` with a `+N`/`-N` operand; any `cd` or `pushd` while CDPATH is assigned or exported in the session; and any `eval` whose operands change directory. (11) The registered path rule is extended to the greenness copy. `D:/sb/green/item-<n>`, in every spelling the BrittonGames rule covers, is replaced by `<greenness-copy>`. The measured spellings `D:/sb/green` and `D:\sb\green` become leak tokens, in the driver's precheck and in the fixture. The five review commits, the fixture and the dry run's fixture are rebuilt with the extended rule. The dry run's review repository reproduces unchanged. Greenness is not re-run, because only `headless.log` changes and neither the build nor the suite reads it.

*(dated 2026-10-10, operator sitting)* **The operator's rulings after refuting round 13** (taken by the operator on 2026-10-09 and 2026-10-10; recorded here, not proposed). (12) The audit is fail-closed. It resolves what it can, and voids at runtime, not only in shadow, any read-position word or cwd move it cannot resolve to a definite path: a backslash escape or a `$'…'`/`$"…"` quote it does not decode (bash quoting is lexed on the raw command before any `\` to `/` fold, and simple `$'…'` escapes are decoded); a `~name` tilde other than the account's own user (`~<user>`, the home directory's last segment, folds to home); an unquoted glob (`*`, `?`, `[`) in a word not anchored inside the copy (a glob whose literal prefix resolves inside the copy is fine: `ls *.cpp`, `grep -rn X --include=*.h`, `find . -name '*.uasset'`); any write to HOME, USERPROFILE, OLDPWD or CDPATH by a means the audit does not model exactly (`printf -v`, `read`, `mapfile`/`readarray`, `declare -n`); a reassigned HOME followed by `~` or a bare `cd`; any `pushd` or `popd` carrying `-n`; `eval`, `source` or `.` of content the audit cannot see; a `cd` or `pushd` inside a shell function or with an unresolved target; and a word whose resolved form still holds a literal `$`. The contract: every read the audit can resolve is checked against the roots and names; every read it cannot resolve voids. (13) The rust-reviewer's F1 to F5 are fixed and F6 to F8 are skipped: F1, `pushd`/`popd -n` and `printf -v OLDPWD`, under (12); F2, the OLDPWD wiring check runs where it is read; F3, CDPATH removed at both spawn sites, with a stub assertion; F4, `source` or `.` of a file the audit did not see written voids; F5, `${OLDPWD…}` parameter operations void. Under H-12 the cwd test's control k7 (`popd -n`) becomes a voiding case; approved. (14) Under H-12, the further expectations ruling (12) moves are approved: in `an_unresolved_var_in_a_read_position_is_flagged_not_voided`, 8 cases now void and 2 now clear, the name kept; and the `$OLDPWDX` and `${OLDPWD_X}` controls now void. (15) D21153-8i-J's voids beyond (12)'s list are accepted: Windows 8.3 short names, chained `git`/`tar`/`make -C` outside the copy, absolute or `..` brace alternatives, `xargs` over unreadable input, and every `~` after a prefix `HOME=` assignment. The false-void calibration over every real transcript (326 tool uses) found 0 false voids. (16) D21153-8i-J's disclosed residuals are accepted and recorded in METHOD-S: junction, symlink and hard-link aliasing (the audit is lexical); a program's own directory option other than `git`/`tar`/`make -C` and `env -C`; interpreter inline code and a child shell's unread script or stdin, which the shadow audit flags. A later round re-raises one only with a concrete exploit.

*(dated 2026-10-10, operator sitting)* **The operator's ruling after refuting round 14** (taken by the operator on 2026-10-10; recorded here, not proposed). (17) The audit's threat model is the honest, curious reviewer. It must void every out-of-copy read an honest reviewer would plausibly make (every real void so far: git in the main repository, `ls /d/sb`, `find /d`, a cd into BrittonGames), stay fail-closed on ordinary shell it cannot resolve, and not false-void honest in-copy work. Deliberate obfuscation (bash escape tricks, deep nesting, Windows path-canonicalisation spellings) is outside the threat model and is a disclosed residual in METHOD-S, except where closing it is cheap and fail-closed. D21153-8i-K's robustness bounds, cheap closes and false-void fixes are accepted, together with the disclosed false-void surface and residuals in METHOD-S's D21153-8i-K extension, items 5 and 6. Those include an unclosed-quote command voiding, a cd into a listing's region voiding (under ruling (12)), and `$(…)..$(…)` voiding. The two unobservable mutant survivors, K-N2 and K-V2, are accepted as recorded. On round 14's 460-command honest set, D21153-8i-K voids 1 (a genuine read of the copy's parent), against 37 under D21153-8i-J; the real transcripts show 0 false voids. Later refuting rounds raise a finding only with an honest-looking command.

*(dated 2026-10-11, operator sitting)* **The operator's ruling after refuting round 15** (taken by the operator on 2026-10-11; recorded here, not proposed). (18) D21153-8i-L's round-15 fixes under rulings (12) and (17) are accepted: a double-quoted `${…}` whose body is not a plain parameter is audited again; the per-command variable copy and the rescans are gone, the cwd is bounded at 1,024 bytes and the pushd stack at 64, and past either the command voids; a read rooted at `/` voids; revision ranges are no read, and `rev-list` with a text format is not a revision; `powershell` is an interpreter, and an option's value is never its script; staged and fed text reads backslashes as separators, and a staged script's `..` climbs from its cwd; a staged text holding a reserved mark voids when run; `read` and `mapfile` fed by `< <(…)`, a here-string or a loop's `done` redirect bind as a pipe does; and a glob in a temp subdirectory the session wrote is its own. No H-12 expectation moved. The real transcripts show 0 false voids; round 15's honest sets void 26 times under D21153-8i-K and 14 under D21153-8i-L; and no must-void case of rounds 12 to 15 is lost. The new residuals in METHOD-S's D21153-8i-L extension, items 3 and 4, are accepted, including a preload option's value run as code (`node -r`, `ruby -r`, `perl -M`; shadow-flagged, not voided) and the rust-reviewer's F4 (arithmetic `$((…))` nesting and parameter writes in heredoc bodies) as obfuscation-class. The robustness test's 5 s bound is kept unchanged: its worst shape takes about 2.7 s when run alone, as the B0 block runs it.

*(dated 2026-10-11, operator sitting)* **The operator's ruling after refuting round 16** (taken by the operator on 2026-10-11; recorded here, not proposed). (19) D21153-8i-M's bounds under rulings (17) and (18) are accepted. The audit counts its own work on each tool use, and a use past 2^24 units voids and flags `audit work bound`. The tracked variables keep an undo log, capped at 1 MiB, past which an assignment binds the unknown value. Subshells nested past 64 void. A process substitution is recorded only after a `<` redirect, at most 64, past which the command voids. A run already read in the same state is replayed, not read again, and `read_feeds` is one pass. Under H-12, the robustness test's 5 s wall-clock bound is replaced by a work count, approved on 2026-10-11 ("Count work, not time"): each shape's work at twice its size is under three times its work at its size, and under the bound. No verdict moved: 1,971 calibrated rows read identically under D21153-8i-L and D21153-8i-M, and round 16's shapes are bounded. The residuals and false-void surface in METHOD-S's D21153-8i-M extension, item 3, are accepted: name listings from `git grep -l`, `git lfs ls-files -n` and `git ls-tree --name-only`; `xargs` fed by a here-string, a heredoc, `< <(…)` or a file written but not staged; `node` `require('../x')` resolved from the cwd; `${arr[$i]}` and `${1:-default}` in double quotes; `mktemp -d` inside a longer word; the outputs of `git format-patch -o`, `split` and `csplit` in a session temp directory; and a work unit's varying cost, up to about 45 s at the bound in the debug build.

*(dated 2026-10-11, operator sitting)* **The operator's ruling after refuting round 17** (taken by the operator on 2026-10-11; recorded here, not proposed). (20) D21153-8i-N's fixes under rulings (16), (17) and (19) are accepted. Under H-12 the robustness test's expectation changes, approved: (i) each shape's work at twice its size is strictly under three times its work at its size, the 4,096-unit allowance in D21153-8i-M's code (`at_2n <= 3 * at_n + 4096`) removed; (ii) two shapes, a command over the audit's size bound (refused unread at a fixed 46 units) and a name at the brace scan's bound (its size is the bound itself; at twice it is the next shape, which the ratio measures), are asserted at their size only, under an absolute ceiling of 4,096 units, instead of by the ratio. Round 17's three honest false-void classes are cleared: a sed script, an awk program and perl's bundled `-ne`-style inline code are code, audited as before but without Win32's spelling closes, their climbing tokens resolved against the cwd, their files read as before; an arithmetic expansion's command substitutions are audited as commands and its value is a count, and `((…))` where a command starts is arithmetic. Round 17's honest sets void 34 times under D21153-8i-M and 6 under D21153-8i-N (13 sed or awk, 12 arithmetic and 3 perl cleared); the 1,971 calibrated rows read identically and no must-void case moved. The rust-reviewer's F1 (digests keyed at random once per audit), F2 (the memo bounded at 2^24 bytes as well as 4,096 outcomes) and F3 (a staged text's copy charged as work) are accepted; F5 to F7 are not taken. The residuals in METHOD-S's D21153-8i-N extension, item 3, are accepted: a UNC, device, trimmed-segment or stream spelling inside code is not closed; `((` other than where a command starts, `for ((…))` included, reads as two subshells; and `set --` positional parameters, `expr $(…)` and a `cd` into a listing's region still void (round 17's six left).

*(dated 2026-10-12, operator sitting)* **The operator's ruling after refuting round 18** (taken by the operator on 2026-10-12; recorded here, not proposed). (21) D21153-8i-O's fixes under rulings (17) and (20) are accepted: the rust-reviewer's N1, an arithmetic body holding a top-level `'` or `$'` voids, flagged "a quote in arithmetic", because bash runs a substitution inside it before rejecting the quote; N2, narrowed to perl, so that in perl's inline code an unquoted bare `..` is the range or flip-flop operator while in every other code, and when quoted in any code, `..` stays the parent (the all-code rule let round 18's must-void `pwsh -c 'gci ..'` through); N3, the root spelled with trailing dots is the root, in code and outside it; and round 18's code-classes finding 1, a climb glued to sed's `r`/`R` is resolved from where it starts and perl's `q{}`/`q[]` split code, of the named raw delimiters only `[` kept. No test expectation moves under H-12; the selection stays 36 tests. The 1,971 calibrated rows and round 17's 2,035 read identically, with 0 false voids on the real transcripts; of round 18's 635 rows, 18 must-void rows newly void and no honest row moves. The residuals and false-void surface in METHOD-S's D21153-8i-O extension, item 3, are accepted, including the fail-open residuals `xargs -a <(…)` / `--arg-file <(…)` and a sed run by find's `-exec` with a glued climb. The rust-reviewer's N4 to N6 are not taken.

*(dated 2026-10-12, operator sitting)* **The operator's ruling after refuting round 19** (taken by the operator on 2026-10-12; recorded here, not proposed). (22) D21153-8i-P's fixes under rulings (17) and (21) are accepted. The first is the rust-reviewer's O1 and O2 on D21153-8i-O and round 19's o-correctness major: in perl's inline code a bare `..` inside a string, a backtick or a quote-like span (`q`, `qq`, `qx`, `qw`) is the parent, and outside every span it is the range or flip-flop operator, whatever stands beside it; a span's closer ends a token, so `q{..}` and `q[..]` read the parent, and the code now implements ruling (21)'s words. The second is round 19's two arithmetic minors: a quote inside `${…}` in arithmetic is honest unless its single-quoted span holds `$(` or a backtick, and bash's `$[…]` is arithmetic (audited; a top-level quote voids; unclosed voids). The rust-reviewer's O3 is not taken, because restricting the glued climb to sed's `r`/`R` moves round 18's must-void L25. No test expectation moves under H-12, and the selection stays 36 tests. The 1,971 calibrated rows, round 17's 2,035 and round 18's 635 read identically, with 0 false voids on the real transcripts; of round 19's 52 rows, 15 move, each as asked: nine must-void reads newly void, one gains a flag, and five honest rows clear. The packet's rehearsal, owed when drive D: filled, was run after the build-output cleanup: 61 cases, REHEARSE_OK. The residuals and false-void surface in METHOD-S's D21153-8i-P extension, item 3, are accepted: O3's regex surface in perl and python code, a quote that opens no perl string, perl heredocs and interpolation, and method or qualified quote-likes.
